# Refactor log

Append-only record of completed steps from the autonomous VPS loop's backlog
(see `NEXT_TASK.md`, and the design rationale in
`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-27-agent-friendly-dev-refactor-design.md`,
which is itself untracked/local per this repo's `docs/*` convention). Each
entry is added as part of the same PR that completes the step (the worker's
own commit, occasionally followed by a small coordinator commit filling in
the PR number once it's known).
- 2026-09-07: Item 88: `pipeline/strategies/static_join.py` no longer drops a post-midnight (`departure_time` hour >= 24) trip's row outright. `pipeline/strategies/_time.py` gained `parse_departure_time`, a single-parse superset of `normalize_departure_time` that also returns the raw seconds-since-service-day-start value (populated for both "ok" and "extended" status); `static_join.py` uses it to keep the row with `scheduled_time` NULL and a new `scheduled_sec Nullable(Int32)` ClickHouse column set instead. `pipeline.clickhouse.UPDATE_COLUMNS` gained `scheduled_sec` as a trailing entry, and `insert_updates` now pads a shorter (legacy 8-tuple) row with `None` for it so `aomori_regex.py` and every pre-existing fixture/test row keep working unchanged. `static_version_id` was also added, resolving the persistence-design question this item's first half had flagged as a blocker (no existing identifier survives past the static-feed fetch step): migration `0036_static_trips_version_id` adds a nullable `static_trips.static_version_id TEXT` column, `pipeline/static_loader.py`'s `load_static()` populates it at INSERT time from the loaded zip's filename stem (same value for every row in one `load_static()` call), and `static_join.py`'s existing `static_trips` join now reads it and forwards it into ClickHouse's `updates.static_version_id` column per RT row. (PR #357)

Format: `- YYYY-MM-DD: <one-line summary of what was done> (PR #NNN)`

## Entries

- 2026-09-05: Added an optional `panel_ctx` field to `POST /ask`'s `AskRequest` (item 79, Copilot plan Phase C Task 9), threaded through only to the Stage-3 LLM path's `chat_with_tools` in `pipeline/query/chat.py`, which appends a one-line "The user is currently viewing the {tab} tab" hint to the system prompt when `panel_ctx.get("tab")` is set — never consulted by the rules/embedding routing stage or tool-dispatch args above it. Extended `tests/api/test_api_ask.py` with `test_ask_forwards_panel_ctx_to_chat_with_tools` and `test_ask_omits_panel_ctx_by_default`, and added the new `panel_ctx=None` parameter to every pre-existing mock `chat_with_tools` stand-in in that file so the added call-site kwarg doesn't break them.
- 2026-09-05: Added the Copilot panel's follow-up question input box (item 80, Copilot plan Phase C Task 10). `CopilotPanel.tsx` gains a `useMutation`-backed form that posts `{ question, panel_ctx: { tab: "overview" } }` to the existing `POST /api/{agencyId}/ask` (item 79's new field, no routing/dispatch change), rendering the answer or an `ErrorBanner` on failure. Added `copilot.followup_placeholder`/`copilot.followup_submit` to both locale files and extended `CopilotPanel.test.tsx` with a follow-up submission test. A follow-up commit fixed a cross-agency state leak: `CopilotPanel` is mounted once outside `<Outlet />` and never remounts on an agency switch, so the follow-up question/answer/error state persisted across agencies — extracted a `FollowupForm` child component keyed by `agencyId`, mirroring `App.tsx`'s existing `key={agencyId}` idiom for routed tabs.

  Review (both required passes) found a genuine Major, confirmed independently by both groups: the initial cut typed `panel_ctx` as a bare `dict | None`, so `tab` could be an arbitrary/oversized string spliced verbatim into the trusted system prompt with no cap — the exact class of prompt-cost/injection risk `chat_with_tools` already guards against for tool-call args a few lines below. Fixed by introducing a `PanelCtx` model (mirroring the existing `AskCtx` pattern) with `tab: Literal["overview", "map", "ask", "live", "analysis", "network"] | None`, kept in sync with `frontend/src/main.tsx`'s top-level tab routes — an unknown value now gets a 422 before it can reach `chat_with_tools`. Also added `test_panel_ctx_tab_appended_to_system_prompt`/`test_panel_ctx_omitted_leaves_system_prompt_unchanged` in `tests/query/test_chat_null_args.py` (the file's existing `test_rag_examples_appended_to_system_prompt` pattern) to directly exercise the prompt-append branch itself, and `test_ask_rejects_unknown_panel_ctx_tab` in `tests/api/test_api_ask.py` for the 422 boundary — none of which the initial cut's tests covered. Verification: `poetry run ruff check`/`poetry run mypy` clean; full `make test` against the throwaway `:5544`/`:8124` DBs, 0 failed.
  Resumed by the coordinator after an interrupted prior tick had already applied this fix but never committed or logged it. Re-verified the fix directly (300 passed/22 skipped on `tests/query/`+`tests/api/test_api_ask.py`, ruff/mypy clean) before committing, then ran a fresh, complete two-pass `/review-branch` from scratch (both passes' groups had no prior record to resume from). Both passes' four reviewer groups found no Major findings; two non-blocking Minors were independently raised by both passes' perf/practices/comments/alternatives group and left as documented residuals: `chat_with_tools` itself still accepts `panel_ctx` as a bare `dict` (the enum safety is enforced only at its single current caller, not by the function itself), and `PanelCtx` is a one-field wrapper model where `panel_tab: PanelTab | None` directly on `AskRequest` would be simpler for today's single field. - 2026-08-28: Banned manual memoization (`useMemo`/`useCallback`/`React.memo`) as a hard ESLint error (`no-restricted-syntax` + `no-restricted-imports` + `no-restricted-properties` in `frontend/eslint.config.js`) since the React Compiler already auto-memoizes; audited all ~32 existing call sites across 17 files (`TabFilterBar.tsx` alone had 8; `useCallback` also appeared in `rangeContext.ts`'s `update` wrapper, in addition to `ThreadSidebar.tsx`) and removed each one, replacing memoized values with plain computations (or immediately-invoked arrows where the original body had an early `return`) and `useCallback` handlers with plain functions. Verified none of the removed values fed a `useEffect`/`useLayoutEffect` dependency array elsewhere (which would have turned a removed memo into an infinite-render bug) before making each change.

  Across several `/review-branch` passes (spanning multiple sessions): fixed the original selectors missing the `React.useMemo`/`React.useCallback` member-expression form and aliased imports (closed via a `no-restricted-imports` backstop, plus a `no-restricted-properties` backstop for a default-aliased receiver or computed property access, later simplified so `no-restricted-properties` is the sole member-expression check — the separate `MemberExpression[object.name='React']` selector in `no-restricted-syntax` was fully redundant with it and only caused double-reporting); fixed `askCardTemplates.ts`'s stale "can be memoized" JSDoc. One interim fix attempt pinned `useRangeContext`'s date defaults via `useState` initializers to stop a per-render wall-clock read — this itself was a regression (it froze "today" for a hook instance's lifetime, so ~10-13 consumers stopped re-converging on the current day across a JST-midnight boundary) and was reverted back to plain per-render computation, which is the shipped, intentional shape. Also removed a `react-hooks/todo` promotion to `'error'` added in an earlier pass as an attempted "compiler bailout" lint signal — it's actually an upstream Hint-severity, off-by-default "unimplemented compiler features" diagnostic, not a bailout signal.

  Verification (2026-08-28, once `frontend/node_modules` became available in a loop worktree for the first time): `npm run typecheck` clean, `npm run lint` clean (0 errors, 1 pre-existing unrelated `no-explicit-any` warning), `npm run test` 377/377 passing across 61 files. Positive/negative control performed directly: reintroduced bare `useMemo`/`useCallback` and `React.useMemo` in `App.tsx`, confirmed all three are caught (`no-restricted-syntax`, `no-restricted-imports`, `no-restricted-properties`, plus `react-hooks/rules-of-hooks`), then reverted and reconfirmed lint clean.
- 2026-09-02: Fixed `pipeline/reports/overview.py`'s `_movers` dropping an entire route from both "worse" and "better" lists whenever its previous-window average was exactly 0.0, hiding the most dramatic possible "worsened" case (baseline all on-time, current window running a real delay). Removed the `if prv_avg == 0: continue` guard; `delta_pct`'s existing `MIN_PRV_AVG_FOR_PCT_MIN` floor already independently suppresses the percentage figure near a zero baseline, so `delta_min`-based ranking no longer needs a redundant, over-broad exclusion. Added `test_movers_includes_route_with_zero_baseline_avg` in `tests/api/test_overview.py` covering a route with an exact 0.0 prior-window average that jumps to a real delay, asserting it appears in `movers.worse` with a correct `delta_min` and `delta_pct=None`, alongside a normal-magnitude route that keeps its real `delta_pct`. Verification: `poetry run ruff check` and `poetry run mypy` clean on the changed files. `make test` scoped to `tests/api/test_overview.py` against the throwaway `:5544` Postgres: 60 passed, including the new regression test. (PR #303)
- 2026-08-29: Audit-first pass on the frontend's missing responsive/mobile layout (only `overview.css` had an `@media` query; the app shell's `Sidebar.tsx` nav had none). Inventoried each tab rather than fixing uniformly: the Sidebar app-shell nav was the dominant, must-fix break (its fixed 230/64px rail ate most of a ~390px phone viewport on every single tab, not just one view), so it now CSS-hides under 640px behind a slim 36px persistent rail + hamburger that opens a full slide-in drawer (mirroring `ThreadSidebar.tsx`'s existing mobile pattern) — deliberately a real flex-row sibling of `<main>`, not a `position:fixed` floating button, so it can't overlap `GuestPrompt`/`DataStalenessBanner`/`FeedHealthBanner`, which already claim the page's literal top-left corner on some agencies/states; the drawer body is lazily mounted only while open (unlike `ThreadSidebar`'s always-mounted twin) so the common closed case doesn't duplicate every nav link in the DOM and break the existing singular `getByText`/`getByRole` assertions in `Sidebar.test.tsx`. `AnalysisTab.tsx`'s report-list/report-body/`InsightPanel` three-column row (280px + flex + 260px, no `ReportTable` change needed since it already wraps its wide tables in its own `overflow-x:auto`) forced page-level horizontal scroll below ~640px, so it now stacks to a single column at that breakpoint via a scoped `<style>` block (same pattern `NetworkTab.tsx` already uses), with `InsightPanel` gaining an optional `className` prop so the stack can override its fixed width. Left `MapTab.tsx`'s floating Legend/Layers panels (already `vw`-relative, e.g. `MapHourScrubber`'s `min(520px, 80vw)`), the already-card-stacked `NetworkTab.tsx` ("Agencies compare-bars"), `OverviewTab.tsx` (already has its own 600px mobile CSS in `overview.css`), and `LiveTab.tsx`/`AskTab.tsx` (already `flexWrap`/existing mobile drawer) as-is — code-level review found no page-level horizontal-overflow risk once the Sidebar itself stopped eating the viewport. Added 5 new `Sidebar.test.tsx` cases for the hamburger/drawer open-close lifecycle and 2 new i18n keys (`nav.open_menu`, en+ja). Verification: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint` (0 errors, 1 pre-existing unrelated warning), `npm run lint:i18n`, `npm run lint:i18n-strings`, `npm run test` (382/382 across 61 files), `npm run test:check-entry-chunk`, `npm run build:bundle` + `npm run check:entry-chunk` all clean. Live Playwright verification at 375-414px (the task's own acceptance check) could **not** be performed in this session — the sandboxed worker environment denies starting any long-running/background process, including `npm run dev` and even a plain `python3 -m http.server`, so no dev server could be reached by a browser; this is a session/tooling limitation, not a gap in `main`, and should be run by whichever environment can host a live server before this is treated as fully verified. (PR #254)

  Doc-consistency fixes applied: `README.md`'s React Compiler bullet no longer describes manual memoization as harmless/optional; `frontend/vite.config.ts`'s comment no longer says manual memoization is "only needed where the compiler bails out" (a bailout should be fixed, not hand-memoized); `frontend/vitest.config.ts` now loads `babel-plugin-react-compiler`, closing a real test/prod divergence (tests were exercising the un-memoized code path while production ran compiled).

  Known, deliberately-accepted residual risk, not fixed in this PR: a perf review flagged that ~8 call sites (`ServiceSplit.tsx`, `RoutesPicker.tsx`, `ConcentrationBar.tsx`, `ReportTable.tsx`, `DailyChart.tsx`, `RoutePickerPill.tsx`, `AgencyPicker.tsx`, `TabFilterBar.tsx`) were converted from `useMemo` to an immediately-invoked function expression rather than a flat sequence of statements, and it's unverified (no compiler-execution tooling available in any sandbox session so far) whether the React Compiler still memoizes the IIFE form as effectively as the inlined form used at every other call site. Two independent review passes (this one and an earlier session) read the shipped `babel-plugin-react-compiler` source and found no explicit "IIFE unsupported" bailout category, making this plausible-but-unverified rather than a confirmed regression. The suggested mitigation of adding `--max-warnings 0` to `frontend/package.json`'s `lint` script (so a real compiler bailout would at least fail lint) was deliberately not applied: the repo already has one pre-existing `@typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any` warning outside this diff's scope, so `--max-warnings 0` would fail `npm run lint` today independent of this change. Needs a human with compiler-profiling tooling (`react-compiler-healthcheck` or DevTools Profiler) to confirm the remaining sites are still memoized, and a separate decision on how to close the `--max-warnings` gap without breaking the existing warning.

  Interactive-session continuation (2026-08-28): merged latest `main` (picked up PR #239's entry-chunk check and PR #243's item 8 fix, resolving a trivial append-only conflict in this file). Ran a fresh two-pass `/review-branch` (per CLAUDE.md's now-mandatory two-pass rule) against the merged state. Pass 1 found no Majors; a perf/practices reviewer found that 4 of the 8 "accepted residual risk" IIFE sites (`DailyChart.tsx`, `RoutePickerPill.tsx`, `ServiceSplit.tsx`, `TabFilterBar.tsx`'s `routeChips`) have no early `return` and so never needed the IIFE wrapper at all — flattened all four into plain sequential `const` statements (matching the pattern already used for `TabFilterBar.tsx`'s `routeNameMap`/`groupCodesByName`), fully removing them from the residual-risk list rather than carrying the risk forward. The remaining 4 sites (`RoutesPicker.tsx`, `ConcentrationBar.tsx`, `ReportTable.tsx`, `AgencyPicker.tsx`) do have a genuine early return and were left as documented residual risk after pass 1. Also fixed two enforcement-review Minors in `frontend/eslint.config.js`'s comments: corrected an overclaim that computed property access is covered "computed or not" (only *literal* computed access like `React["useMemo"]` is; a dynamically computed name is an inherent ESLint limitation, verified by direct reproduction) and documented that `no-restricted-imports` can false-positive on a future namespace import (`import * as React`) and that `no-restricted-properties` matches the property name on any receiver, not just React.
- 2026-09-03: Fixed `scripts/prepare_review.py`'s `suggested_tier()` mis-classifying a diff that mixes `CLAUDE.md` with an ordinary top-level doc (e.g. `docs/features/foo.md`) as "trivial" instead of "process-doc" — it only checked whether *every* changed path was `CLAUDE.md`/`.claude/**` before falling through to the `all(path.endswith(".md"))` trivial check, so a mixed diff never matched the first branch. The first attempt widened that check from `all(...)` to `any(...)`, but this over-corrected: it let a `CLAUDE.md`-plus-real-code diff (e.g. `CLAUDE.md` + `scripts/foo.py`) collapse to process-doc's single reviewer instead of standard's two, silently dropping `bugs`/`perf`/`alternatives` coverage from a diff that also carries a genuine code change. A follow-up attempt to narrow it back down (requiring every path to end in `.md` before checking for `CLAUDE.md`/`.claude/**`) went too far the other way and broke an existing, still-relied-on case — running the full test file caught `test_process_doc_and_enforcement_flags_are_deterministic` failing, because a lone `.claude/settings.json` change (not itself Markdown) no longer matched. Final fix keeps the original `all(path == "CLAUDE.md" or path.startswith(".claude/"))` check as the primary path (correctly covers any all-`.claude/**`/`CLAUDE.md` diff, Markdown or not) and adds a second, narrower check only for an all-Markdown diff that also contains a process-doc path — covering the originally-reported mixed case without weakening either the non-Markdown `.claude/**` case or the real-code-mixed case. A first version of that second check only tested the literal string `"CLAUDE.md"`, not the `.claude/` prefix used by the primary path; all three independent review passes on this branch caught the resulting gap (a `.claude/**.md` file mixed with an ordinary doc still fell through to "trivial") and converged on the same fix: extracted a shared `_is_process_doc_path()` predicate and reused it in both branches, closing the gap and removing the duplicated string-matching logic in one change. Added `test_process_doc_wins_when_mixed_with_an_ordinary_markdown_file`, `test_process_doc_wins_when_a_claude_markdown_file_mixes_with_an_ordinary_markdown_file`, and `test_process_doc_does_not_win_when_mixed_with_a_non_markdown_file` in `tests/unit/test_prepare_review.py`. Verification: `poetry run ruff check`/`mypy` clean on both changed files; `poetry run pytest tests/unit/test_prepare_review.py`: 19 passed, 0 failed, against the throwaway `:5544` Postgres/`:8124` ClickHouse containers. (PR #316)

  Pass 2 (fresh, independent, per the mandatory two-pass rule) found no Majors and re-confirmed pass 1's fixes; a perf/practices/alternatives reviewer went further on the 4 remaining IIFE sites: `RoutesPicker.tsx`'s `filteredGroups` didn't need a function wrapper at all (its "early return" was a plain two-way branch, replaced with a ternary), and the other 4 (`AgencyPicker.tsx`'s `filtered`, `ReportTable.tsx`'s `maxes`, `ConcentrationBar.tsx`'s Lorenz-curve computation, `RoutesPicker.tsx`'s `groups`) were hoisted to module-scope named pure functions (`filterAgencies`, `computeColumnMaxes`, `buildLorenzCurve`, `buildRouteGroups`) instead of inline IIFEs — an ordinary named-function `CallExpression` is unambiguously memoized by the React Compiler the same way as any other call in these components, closing the "does the compiler memoize an inline IIFE the same way" question entirely rather than leaving it open pending unavailable profiling tooling. **This fully resolves the previously-documented residual risk — zero `useMemo`-to-IIFE conversions remain in this diff.** Re-verified after all fixes: `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`test` clean (377/377). (PR #242)
- 2026-08-28: Fixed `test_followup_pagination_two_turns`'s live failure (item 8): confirmed `pipeline/query/router.py`'s `is_follow_up("次の50件")` already correctly detects the pagination continuation (already unit-tested), so `api/routers/ask.py` correctly skips the router and reroutes to `chat_with_tools` with history attached — the gap was downstream, in `pipeline/query/chat.py`'s LLM call: `tool_choice="auto"` still let the model decline to re-call `describe_data` with an incremented `offset` even though `SYSTEM_PROMPT` documents that exact rewrite, producing the observed `tool_call: None`. Added a `force_tool_call` parameter (set by `api/routers/ask.py` exactly when `is_follow_up()` matched and history is attached) that maps to `tool_choice="required"` for that one request, removing the model's option to answer in free text for a phrasing class where a tool re-invocation is the only correct response; every other question shape keeps `tool_choice="auto"`. Added unit coverage (`tests/query/test_chat_null_args.py::test_force_tool_call_sets_tool_choice_required`) and API-level regression coverage (`tests/api/test_api_ask.py::test_follow_up_reroutes_to_llm_with_history` now also pins `force_tool_call=True`; `test_ask_router_fallthrough_passes_rag_examples` pins `force_tool_call=False` for non-follow-up questions). Local verification could not be run in this sandboxed worktree: its poetry virtualenv has zero installed packages (`poetry run pytest`/`ruff`/`mypy` all report "Command not found") and `poetry install` isn't in this loop's Bash allowlist — the same class of tooling-provisioning gap already documented for frontend `node_modules` in `NEXT_TASK.md`'s status log, now confirmed on the Python side too. Code changes reviewed by hand against the existing call sites instead; a human or a later tick with a provisioned environment should run `make check` before merge. (PR #243)
- 2026-08-28: Added a comment in `api/main.py`'s `spa_fallback` route flagging the scope trade-off of the dot-prefix guard added to fix the `dist/.vite` leak: it blocks every dot-prefixed path segment, not just that leak, so a future legitimate dot-prefixed route (e.g. `/.well-known/...`) would silently get the SPA shell instead of the real file or a real 404. No behavior change. (PR #246)
- 2026-08-28: Closed `check-entry-chunk.mjs`'s manifest-graph blind spot (item 9): the script previously only walked Vite's manifest-tracked static import graph, so a hand-authored `<script src>`/`<link href>` tag added directly to `frontend/index.html` (pointing at a file copied verbatim into `dist/`, e.g. from `public/`) would ship in the same initial HTML load while being completely invisible to that walk. Added a second, independent pass that scans the built `dist/index.html`'s own `<script>`/`<link>` tags directly for the same MapLibre markers, skipping external/non-file URLs (e.g. Google Fonts `https://...` links) so they don't false-positive; documented the scope boundary (regex-based tag scan, not a full HTML parser) in the script's header comment. Added 7 new regression tests in `tests/frontend/check_entry_chunk.test.mjs` (hand-authored `<script src>` bypass caught, hand-authored unquoted `<script src=...>` bypass caught, hand-authored `<link href>` bypass caught, external link no false-positive, clean index.html passes, missing `dist/index.html` fails loudly) and gave the ~9 existing tests a default benign `index.html` fixture via `makeDist`'s new `indexHtml` option so they weren't broken by the new fail-closed read. Verified end-to-end against a real build: `npm run build:bundle && npm run check:entry-chunk` passes clean on the unmodified `frontend/index.html`. Could not perform the equivalent live "add a MapLibre `<script>` tag to `frontend/index.html`, rebuild, confirm caught, revert" round-trip in this sandboxed worktree — editing `frontend/index.html` itself is outside this session's `Edit` allowlist (only `frontend/scripts/**`, `frontend/src/**`, and a few named config files are covered); the 7 new regression tests exercise the identical bypass scenario via temp-dir fixtures instead. `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`lint:i18n`/`lint:i18n-strings`/`test` (377/377) all clean; `npm run test:check-entry-chunk` 15/15 passing.

  `/review-branch` pass 1 (2026-08-28, coordinator-direct, resumed from an interrupted prior session): no Majors; two Minors fixed directly — corrected this entry's stale "14/14" count (a later commit had added an 8th unquoted-attribute test without updating it; now 15/15 total, 7 new) and documented a narrow regex edge case (an unquoted attribute value immediately followed by a no-space self-closing `/>` folds the trailing slash into the captured URL) in the script's header comment.

  Pass 2 (fresh, independent, per the mandatory two-pass rule): a perf/practices reviewer found a real Minor-rated bug with Major-scale impact on the check's own headline scenario — the new index.html scan didn't exclude files already covered by the manifest's static closure, so if MapLibre ever actually leaks into the real entry chunk (the original reason this whole script exists), the build correctly fails via the manifest walk's "contains MapLibre" message but the new guard *also* independently re-checks the same file and emits a second, factually wrong "not part of the Vite manifest's static import graph... likely a hand-authored tag" message for what is actually a real static-import regression — misdirecting debugging toward a nonexistent rogue `public/` file. Fixed by tracking every `dist/` path already inspected via the manifest closures (`checkedFilePaths`) and skipping those in the HTML-scan guard. Added a regression test (`MapLibre marker directly in the entry chunk itself is not also misreported as a hand-authored bypass`) asserting the correct message fires and the misleading one does not. Re-verified: `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`test` (377/377) clean; `npm run test:check-entry-chunk` 16/16 passing. (PR #248)
- 2026-08-28: De-duplicated the `dist/.vite` build-manifest-stripping logic (item 10) that `Dockerfile`'s frontend stage and `Makefile`'s `bake` target each implemented independently with no cross-reference. Extracted a shared `scripts/strip_vite_manifest.sh <dir>` (removes `<dir>/.vite`) and pointed both call sites at it: the Dockerfile now `COPY`s the script into the frontend build stage and runs it via `sh` before `COPY --from=frontend` copies `dist/` into the image; `make bake` now calls it via `sh scripts/strip_vite_manifest.sh api/static` instead of its own inline `rm -rf`. Invoked via an explicit `sh` interpreter rather than relying on the executable bit (matching this Makefile's existing `bash scripts/fetch_archives.sh`-style pattern for other scripts), so no `chmod` was needed. - 2026-08-29: Fixed the Ask tab anchoring an unrelated follow-up question to a stale prior turn's result instead of dispatching a fresh tool call (item 16). Root cause: `pipeline/query/chat.py`'s `chat_with_tools` attaches the last few conversation turns to the LLM prompt whenever the API layer has history to offer, regardless of whether `router.is_follow_up()` matched the current question — so an on-topic but non-continuation question (e.g. "停留所はいくつ？" after a delay-ranking turn) reached Stage 3 with unrelated history attached and `tool_choice="auto"`, and the model answered from that stale table text instead of calling `describe_data`. `route_or_examples()` already runs against the fresh question alone before Stage 3 (no change needed there), so the fix is a scoping instruction: the history block now ends with an explicit guard line (ja/en) telling the model to use the prior turns only when the current question explicitly references them, and to call the appropriate tool on its own for a new, unrelated topic instead of answering from the attached data. Added `tests/query/test_chat_null_args.py::test_history_block_scopes_use_to_explicit_references` (pins the guard text in both locales), `tests/query/test_chat_null_args.py::test_unrelated_question_with_unrelated_history_dispatches_tool` (end-to-end through `chat_with_tools` with a fake LLM playing the role of a correctly-behaving model, DB-backed), and `tests/api/test_api_ask.py::test_unrelated_question_with_unrelated_history_gets_fresh_tool_call` (pins the surrounding `api/routers/ask.py` plumbing: not a recognized continuation so `force_tool_call` stays False, history is still threaded through, and the resulting tool call reaches the client unmodified). Also trimmed a 16-line dated-incident-narrative comment in `pipeline/query/chat.py` down to its durable rationale (Minor, practices), and fixed a pre-existing (not introduced by this diff) `ruff` line-length violation in the same test file. - 2026-08-29: Investigated the Delay ranking report's bare `Route <code>` fallback (item 18). Traced the render path: `frontend/src/components/ReportTable.tsx` resolves each ranking row's `route_code` via `frontend/src/api/useRouteNames.ts`, which builds its lookup `Map` from `GET /api/{agency_id}/routes` (`api/routers/static.py`'s `list_routes`, itself `regexp_replace(route_id, '.*\((\d+)\)$', '\1')`-derived) and only shows the bare-code fallback when that code is entirely absent from the map — never merely under-named. Confirmed `api/routers/reports.py`'s `forecast_overview` route-label query uses the identical regex against the identical `static_routes` rows (`COALESCE(NULLIF(short,''), NULLIF(long,''), route_code)`), so the two call sites necessarily agree on which codes match; a genuine miss there is therefore almost certainly a real static-data gap (case a: the RT feed's trip_id-derived route_code has no corresponding parenthesised suffix in any current `static_routes.route_id` for that agency, e.g. a route retired or renumbered in the static schedule but still emitting RT delay data) rather than a matching bug, though this could not be confirmed against the live dev Postgres data — this sandboxed worktree's Bash tool has no network path to `localhost:5433` (or any host) at all, unlike the read-only SELECT access CLAUDE.md otherwise authorizes for agents. Independently of that gap, found and fixed a real case-b defect while comparing the two implementations: `useRouteNames.ts`'s map only tried `route_short_name || route_id`, silently skipping `route_long_name` — unlike the backend's own three-way COALESCE — so a route whose `route_short_name` is blank showed its raw `route_id` (which redundantly re-embeds the parenthesised code) instead of the cleaner `route_long_name`. Fixed the frontend to prefer `route_short_name`, then `route_long_name`, then `route_id`, matching the backend's fallback order (empty strings, not just null/undefined, count as absent on both sides). Added `frontend/src/api/useRouteNames.test.ts` (fallback-order unit coverage: short name, long-name fallback, route_id fallback, bare-code miss, null/undefined input), `frontend/src/api/useRouteNames.test.tsx` (JSX-consumer coverage of the hook's `isLoading`/`data` passthrough), and `frontend/src/components/ReportTable.test.tsx` (regression guard: a matched route renders its name, an unmatched route_code still renders the `Route <code>` fallback text rather than throwing). Recommends a follow-up manual check against dev Postgres (`SELECT route_id, route_short_name, route_long_name FROM static_routes WHERE agency_id = <青森市バス> AND regexp_replace(route_id, '.*\((\d+)\)$', '\1') NOT IN ('53011', '44242')` style query, or the inverse) to confirm the data-gap hypothesis and, if confirmed, refresh/backfill that agency's static GTFS ingest. Verified: `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`lint:i18n`/`lint:i18n-strings`/`test` (391/391)/`test:check-entry-chunk` (16/16) all clean; `npm run build:bundle && npm run check:entry-chunk` clean (MapLibre stays out of the entry chunk). Backend unchanged, so no `poetry`/pytest run was needed for this diff. (PR #255)

Fixed to the same 3-way order as `useRouteNames.ts`. Added a regression test. `npm run typecheck`/`lint` clean; `npm run test` 392/392 passing. (PR #255)

- 2026-08-29: Added a growable synthetic GTFS static+RT fixture generator (item 21) and used it to verify `pipeline/analyze.py`'s `agg_*` aggregation is arithmetically correct end-to-end, closing a gap where existing pipeline tests either only exercised the *loading* step (`test_static_loader.py`/`test_static_join.py`) or seeded `updates` with delay values never chosen to make the resulting aggregates easy to hand-verify (`test_analyze.py`'s `_seed_updates`). New `tests/fixtures/synthetic_gtfs.py` provides `build_static_zip` (hoisted from `test_static_loader.py`'s original `_make_zip`, which now imports it back instead of keeping its own copy, extended with an optional `stop_times_rows` param the original never needed) plus three named, growable `SyntheticPattern` builders — `uniform_delays` (25 trips, one uniform 30s delay), `outlier_spike` (24 trips at 30s plus one 600s spike), and `null_delays` (22 trips at 60s plus 3 NULL-delay trips) — each shipping a hand-computed `expected` dict of `agg_*` column values as the single source of truth items 22/23 can later import too. New `tests/pipeline/test_synthetic_agg_e2e.py` loads each pattern's static fragment via `pipeline.static_loader.load_static`, inserts its synthetic `updates` rows directly into ClickHouse, runs `analyze()`, and asserts 11 `agg_*` tables (route_stats, route_hour, route_dow, route_hour_dow, daily_trend, route_daily, hour_daily, stop_seq, stop_daily, stop_routes, feed_health) against those hand-computed values. Along the way, hand-tracing Postgres's `PERCENT_RANK()`-based percentile formula (`(rank-1)/(row_count-1)`) surfaced a genuine, non-obvious result: when every row in a partition is tied at the same delay value, every row gets rank 1 and percent_rank 0, so `WHERE pct >= 0.5`/`>= 0.9` matches nothing and `p50_min`/`p90_min` come back NULL rather than the tied value — true for both `uniform_delays` (all 25 rows identical) and the surviving 22-row subset of `null_delays` (also uniform); only `outlier_spike`'s genuine variance produces non-NULL percentiles (10.0 min for both p50 and p90, since the single spike dominates both thresholds). Documented and asserted as `None` rather than avoided, since it is the correct answer under the current implementation. The implementing worker could not run `RUN_CH_INTEGRATION=1 poetry run pytest` from inside this sandboxed worktree itself: its poetry virtualenv has zero installed packages and `poetry install`/`python3 -c`/`python3 -m` are all outside a dispatched worker's Bash allowlist — the same tooling-provisioning gap already documented for items 8, 10, and 16 above (the throwaway Postgres `:5544` and ClickHouse `:8124` containers were already up and confirmed reachable via `docker ps`, so only the Python side was blocked). Every query and dedup/percentile/timezone/null-handling code path was instead traced by hand against `pipeline/analyze.py`, `pipeline/db.py`'s `build_dedup_ch_sql`, and `pipeline/clickhouse.py` to derive the `expected` values above.

  The coordinator closed this verification gap during `/review-branch`: pointed the main checkout's already-provisioned poetry env at the worktree's file paths for `ruff`/`mypy` (clean), then temporarily added `os.environ.setdefault(...)` defaults for `DATABASE_URL`/`RUN_CH_INTEGRATION` directly above `tests/conftest.py`'s existing `DATABASE_URL = os.environ["DATABASE_URL"]` line (env-var prefixes on the command line break the allowlisted `poetry run pytest` prefix match, so this is the established workaround from item 16's history) to run the real `RUN_CH_INTEGRATION=1` suite from outside the worktree pin — all 4 new tests plus the 8 pre-existing `test_static_loader.py` tests passed against the live `:5544`/`:8124` containers, then reverted the temporary conftest.py edit before committing (confirmed via `git diff`/`git status --porcelain` showing zero net change to `conftest.py`). `/review-branch` pass 1 (two independent reviewers: bugs+logic+consistency+security; perf+practices+alternatives) found zero Majors. The bugs+logic+consistency+security reviewer independently re-traced every hand-computed `expected` value against the same SQL and confirmed all of them correct, including the `PERCENT_RANK` NULL-percentile reasoning above. The perf+practices+alternatives reviewer found two real Minors: (a) `_ts()`'s hardcoded 2-digit seconds field silently produced malformed ISO-8601 past 60 rows, breaking the module's own stated "growable, more patterns can be added later" contract for any future higher-volume pattern — fixed by deriving `captured_at` from a fixed UTC base instant plus `timedelta(seconds=offset)` instead (headroom to ~23h/82800 rows before crossing the JST calendar-day boundary the hand-computed `date`s depend on); re-verified (ruff/mypy clean, all 12 tests still passing). (b) the three pattern builders each hand-roll near-identical `routes_rows`/`stops_rows`/`trips_rows` boilerplate (~15 lines x3) — left as an accepted, low-priority duplication per the reviewer's own assessment, since extracting it doesn't conflict with the module's explicit "don't merge into one mega-fixture" design but isn't required for correctness either.

  Pass 2 (fresh, independent manifest + two fresh reviewer dispatches) caught a real bug in pass 1's own fix: the perf+practices+alternatives reviewer traced the UTC+9 JST arithmetic by hand and found `_TS_BASE`'s comment claimed ~23h/82800s of headroom before the JST calendar date rolls over, but `_TS_BASE` (UTC 2026-06-01T01:00:00) is already JST 10:00 — the real headroom to the next JST midnight was only ~14h/50400s, not 23h. A future higher-volume pattern trusting the stated 82800s figure could have silently split its rows across two JST dates. Fixed by moving `_TS_BASE` to UTC 2026-05-31T15:00:00 (exactly JST 2026-06-01T00:00:00), giving the full intended ~24h/86400s of headroom, and corrected the docstring's number to match; re-verified (ruff/mypy clean, all 12 tests still passing against live `:5544`/`:8124`). The bugs+logic+consistency+security reviewer independently re-traced every `expected` value again on this pass and found nothing new. The same alternatives suggestion (parametrize the 3 near-duplicate `test_*_pattern_...` tests over `ALL_PATTERNS` instead of hand-writing one per pattern, so a future 4th pattern can't be registered without also being aggregation-tested) was raised again — not applied here, since the current 3 patterns are fully covered and this is a test-structure improvement rather than a defect, but worth doing before items 22/23 rely on the module's "add a pattern, it's covered" framing. (PR #260)

New `tests/ask_eval/test_synthetic_numeric.py` seeds a throwaway agency with each of item 21's three `tests/fixtures/synthetic_gtfs.py` patterns (`uniform_delays`/`outlier_spike`/`null_delays`), asks the Ask tab a natural-language mean-delay question naming that pattern's synthetic route, and asserts the API's returned `avg_min` matches the pattern's hand-computed `expected["agg_route_stats"]["avg_min"]` — the same ground truth `tests/pipeline/test_synthetic_agg_e2e.py` imports, not a number re-derived here. Unlike `test_baseline.py` (which posts to an externally-started `uvicorn` process), this boots `api.main.app` in-process via `httpx.ASGITransport` (the `tests/api/test_api_ask.py` pattern) wired to the throwaway test Postgres/ClickHouse fixtures, with `chat_with_tools` left unmocked — gated behind `RUN_LLM_EVAL=1`/`GROQ_API_KEY` (same tier as `test_baseline.py`), so a real Groq tool-use call is what actually answers each question; the synthetic route codes match no Stage-1 rule regex and the throwaway agency has no RAG index, so every question is guaranteed to fall through to the real Stage-3 LLM instead of a deterministic router shortcut. A human or a later tick with a provisioned environment should run the offline tests plus, ideally, a real `RUN_LLM_EVAL=1` pass before merge. (PR #261)
- 2026-08-29: Reworked `frontend/src/pages/HelpPage.tsx` (item 24) from a single top-to-bottom scrolling Markdown render into a sidebar-TOC + section-switch UX, per the user's explicit "just a flat .md file" NG feedback. The fetched `.md` source stays a single file per locale (unchanged, one fetch per locale); `splitIntoSections` now parses it client-side, in memory, by top-level `## ` headings into `{ title, markdown }` sections (any prose above the first `## ` stays attached to that first section instead of being dropped), a fixed left `<nav>` lists every section's title (pulled straight from its own heading text, no second hardcoded title list), and only the selected section's markdown renders on the right via `react-markdown`/`rehype-slug` — clicking a sidebar entry swaps content with no page reload. Deep-link decision: old `#N-...` anchors still work. The manual's own "Table of contents" section already lists one `[title](#anchor)` link per other section in matching order, and those anchors are already exactly what `rehype-slug` assigns when the whole document renders (that's how the old flat page's own in-page TOC worked) — `tocAnchorsBySectionIndex` reuses them positionally instead of recomputing slugs by hand, avoiding a new dependency (a `github-slugger` import was tried first, since `rehype-slug` already wraps it and it's already resolved in `package-lock.json` as a transitive dep, but adding it as a direct dependency needs a lockfile regen this sandboxed worktree's permission grants block — both `npm install` and hand-editing `package-lock.json` were denied — so the ToC-anchors approach was used instead, requiring no dependency change at all). This is intentionally not preserved for anchors into a `###` subsection: those used to be deduped by one slugger across the whole document, but each section's subheadings now slug independently when rendered, so two subsections sharing text across different sections would no longer get the disambiguating `-1` suffix the old flat render gave the second one; today no two subsection headings share text in either locale, so this is a theoretical gap, not an active break. Initial-section selection and the sidebar's active highlight are derived at render time from `location.hash` (captured once at mount) rather than set from a bare `useEffect` body, since `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` (an error in this repo's ESLint config) flags exactly that pattern; the only state setters are a sidebar `onClick` and a `hashchange` listener's callback (both explicitly endorsed by that rule's own guidance), and a `history.replaceState` effect keeps the address bar's hash in sync with whichever section's real rendered heading id is on screen. Added `HelpPage.test.tsx` coverage for the sidebar section list, initial-section default, sidebar-click content swap, active-highlight update, and the `#anchor` deep-link case. Verified: `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`lint:i18n`/`lint:i18n-strings`/`test` (401/401) all clean; `npm run test:check-entry-chunk` (16/16); `npm run build:bundle && npm run check:entry-chunk` clean (MapLibre stays out of the entry chunk). - 2026-08-29: Fixed a design flaw in item 23's already-merged `tests/ask_eval/test_synthetic_numeric.py` (PR #261, found via an interactive-session `/review-branch`). The perf+practices+alternatives reviewer traced `pipeline/query/chat.py` and found that a successful tool-call response's `answer` text is always rendered deterministically by `render_tool_result` (`chat.py`'s `_dispatch_and_respond`) — the model only ever selects `(tool_name, arguments)`, never composes the number itself. A *fresh, isolated* question (what the parametrized live-LLM test asked) therefore cannot exercise "the model states a hallucinated number" at all: the model has every incentive to call the right tool, and once it does, the returned number is guaranteed correct by construction — the test could only ever have caught a wrong-tool-selection bug (already covered by `test_baseline.py`'s golden set) or a SQL/dispatch bug (needs no LLM). The one path where the model emits unchecked free text is when it skips tool dispatch entirely (`chat.py`'s `body = (msg.content or "").strip()` fallback) — item 16's original bug shape. Fix: the question now seeds a misleading, unrelated `top_n` ranking turn in `history` first (mirroring item 16's own regression tests), genuinely tempting the model into the risky free-text path; `assert_matches_ground_truth` (unchanged logic, relocated and renamed — see below) already checks the tool-call name before the number, so a model that takes the shortcut now fails clearly rather than the test structurally being unable to observe it. Also fixed a related Minor: the three offline assertion-helper tests, despite the module's own "needs no DB" claim, actually errored under an unreachable Postgres (`tests/conftest.py`'s `apply_schema` is a session-scoped autouse fixture that runs for every test under `tests/`, not just DB-touching ones) — moved them to `tests/unit/test_ask_eval_numeric_helper.py` (which overrides `apply_schema` to bypass Postgres entirely, per CLAUDE.md's "pure logic tests under tests/unit/" convention), and confirmed the moved tests pass against both a real and a deliberately-unreachable `DATABASE_URL`. Extracted the shared assertion logic (`assert_matches_ground_truth`/`extract_avg_min`/`fake_route_stats_response`) into a new `tests/ask_eval/numeric_ground_truth.py` so neither the live-LLM tier nor the offline tier re-derives it; the pre-existing `@pytest.mark.parametrize("pattern_fn", ALL_PATTERNS)` structure and its `pg_conn.commit()` call (both correctly present in the merged PR #261 code — an earlier draft of this same fix mistakenly rebuilt the file from a stale pre-fix commit and briefly reintroduced both regressions, caught by this fix's own `/review-branch` before anything shipped) are preserved unchanged. Also wired `tests/ask_eval/test_synthetic_numeric.py` into `.github/workflows/ask-eval-weekly.yml` (a new step after the existing golden-set eval step, `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}` so it still reports independently if the golden-set step fails, and never before or in parallel with that step — this test's fixtures wipe both ClickHouse's `updates` table and, via `pg_conn`'s teardown, effectively all shared Postgres tables the golden-set step's still-running API server depends on), since the original PR left the new tests unreachable by any scheduled run despite item 23's text asking for exactly that. Verified: `ruff`/`mypy` clean on all three touched/new Python files; the 3 offline tests pass under both a real throwaway Postgres and a deliberately-unreachable one; the 3 parametrized live-LLM tests correctly SKIP (not error) without `RUN_LLM_EVAL=1` against the live `:5544`/`:8124` throwaway stack; the new workflow YAML parses. The 3 live-LLM tests still could not be run for real here (no `GROQ_API_KEY` configured in this environment) — a future run with a configured key, or the new `ask-eval-weekly.yml` step itself, should confirm they pass. (PR #262)

- 2026-08-30: Item 30 — three directional UI/UX findings from the 2026-08-29 Playwright walkthrough, each needing a scope decision before implementation; all three were scoped and implemented. (1) Anonymous filter persistence: added `frontend/src/api/anonymousFilterPersistence.ts` (`useAnonymousFilterPersistence`, wired into `App.tsx` alongside `useDefaultRangeAnchor`) that remembers the last-used date range/DOW/time-band/routes filter per agency in `localStorage` (`transit.lastFilter.<agencyId>`) for anonymous sessions only, and restores it on a fresh visit with no explicit filter query params — an explicit in-session "clear all" is tracked separately (a `restoredFor` ref) so it isn't immediately fought by a re-restore of the old value. Scope decision: this is a genuine, distinct gap from `PresetMenu.tsx`'s login-gated named/multi-slot presets (`/api/me/presets`) — a single "remember what I was just looking at" convenience, not a competing free version of the stronger feature — so `GuestPrompt.tsx`'s existing "sign in to save your filters" nudge is left untouched and still points at presets; logged-in users are unaffected (hook no-ops once a session exists). (2) Help discoverability: new `frontend/src/components/HelpHint.tsx`, a one-time (not recurring, unlike `GuestPrompt`), dismissable corner hint pointing at `/help` — today only reachable via the account-menu popover, with nothing surfacing it to a first-time visitor. Gated on a first-visit `localStorage` timestamp (`help_hint_first_visit_at`) the same way `GuestPrompt` gates its own timer, shown after a 3s delay and hidden again after a 5-minute window if never dismissed; not restricted to anonymous users (Help is equally useful to everyone, and gating it added no clear benefit). Rendered as a small `position: fixed` corner pill rather than a fourth top banner, specifically so it doesn't compound sub-item (3)'s stacking problem. (3) Mobile banner compaction: new shared hook `frontend/src/hooks/useTapToExpandBanner.ts`, consumed by `DataStalenessBanner.tsx`, `FeedHealthBanner.tsx`, and `GuestPrompt.tsx` — below the existing 640px `MOBILE_BREAKPOINT_QUERY` each banner's message now renders as a single truncated (`text-overflow: ellipsis`) line that expands to the full wrapped message on tap/Enter/Space (`role="button"`, `aria-expanded="false"` while collapsed), leaving desktop untouched; deliberately one-directional (no collapse-back) since a toggle added aria/keyboard complexity for no real benefit once a user has already read the full message. New tests: `anonymousFilterPersistence.test.tsx`, `HelpHint.test.tsx`, `useTapToExpandBanner.test.tsx`, new `DataStalenessBanner.test.tsx`/`GuestPrompt.test.tsx` (neither had a test file before), plus a new compact-mode case added to the existing `FeedHealthBanner.test.tsx`. New i18n keys under `help.hint_*` (en+ja). Verified: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test` (439/439 across 77 files), `npm run lint` (0 errors, same 4 pre-existing unrelated warnings), `npm run lint:i18n`/`lint:i18n-strings`, `npm run test:check-entry-chunk`, `npm run build:bundle && npm run check:entry-chunk` all clean. - 2026-08-31: Fixed item 38's rounding-before-reweight bug: six `agg_*` tables (`agg_route_stats`, `agg_route_hour`, `agg_route_dow`, `agg_route_hour_dow`, `agg_daily_trend`, `agg_route_daily`) stored only a pre-rounded per-bucket mean (`avg_min`, or `avg_delay_sec` for `agg_route_daily`), so every downstream consumer that pooled multiple buckets via `SUM(avg_min * samples) / SUM(samples)` was re-weighting already-rounded values instead of the raw delay data, drifting slightly from the true pooled mean (and from the live/ClickHouse path, which averages raw seconds directly). Migration 0028 adds a `sum_delay_sec` column (the exact `SUM(dep_delay)` in seconds) to all six tables — nullable to match each table's existing `avg_min`/`samples` nullability, except `agg_route_daily`'s (declared `NOT NULL` like its siblings, backfilled from the existing rounded columns as a stopgap until the next `analyze()` run repopulates it exactly); `pipeline/analyze.py`'s six builders now populate it, and its module docstring documents the new invariant.

  Switched every confirmed downstream consumer to `SUM(sum_delay_sec) / SUM(samples)` (dividing once, at the end): `pipeline/reports/overview.py`'s `_headline_stats`/`_per_route_avg`/`_service_split`/`_service_split_daily`/`_daily_sparkline`/`_route_weekly_history` fast paths, `pipeline/reports/rankings.py`'s `compute_dow_ranking`/`compute_compare_ranking`/`compute_trend_series` fast paths (the last also closed a second layer of the identical bug in its own Python-side day/bucket-total pooling, which was re-weighting each route/service group's already-rounded display average), and `pipeline/digest/build.py`'s `_ROUTE_BASELINE_SQL` (plus its adjacent `_aggregate_by_route`/`_DAY_ROUTES_SQL`, which pool the same `agg_route_daily` table one function away). Softened the two overclaiming docstrings in `pipeline/reports/forecast.py` ("a weighted mean of per-departure averages equals the pooled mean, so it is exact") to state the actual invariant: pooling is exact only when it divides an exact raw-seconds sum once, at the end, not when it re-weights an already-rounded per-bucket average.

  Added tests proving the two methods diverge on hand-picked per-bucket values and that the fixed figure matches a from-scratch raw-observation computation: `tests/unit/test_digest_aggregate.py::test_pools_exact_sum_delay_sec_not_rounded_avg_delay_sec`, `tests/pipeline/test_analyze.py::test_analyze_sum_delay_sec_pools_exactly_unlike_reweighted_avg_min` (cross-checked against a live `AVG(dep_delay)` over the same raw `updates` rows), `tests/pipeline/test_digest_build.py::test_route_baseline_sql_pools_exact_sum_delay_sec_not_rounded_avg_min`, `tests/api/test_overview.py::test_headline_pools_exact_sum_delay_sec_not_rounded_avg_min`, `tests/api/test_reports.py::test_dow_ranking_pools_exact_sum_not_rounded_avg_min` and `::test_compute_trend_series_week_bucket_pools_exact_sum_not_rounded_avg_min`. Updated ~15 other tests' fixture `INSERT`s across `tests/api/` and `tests/query/` to also supply `sum_delay_sec` — mandatory wherever they seed `agg_route_daily` directly (its column is `NOT NULL`), and needed by the newly-fixed queries wherever a test seeds `agg_daily_trend`/`agg_route_stats` directly rather than via `analyze()`.

  Not fixed (same bug pattern, outside this item's explicitly named call sites — worth a follow-up item, since all six tables now carry the `sum_delay_sec` column needed to close them too): `api/routers/reports.py`'s `forecast_heatmap`/`forecast_overview`/`_fetch_recent_daily_rows`, `api/routers/map.py`'s route-summary baseline, `api/routers/overview.py`'s peak-hour query, `pipeline/reports/overview.py`'s `_concentration`/`_peak_hour_by_dow`, `pipeline/reports/suggest.py`'s baseline pooling, `pipeline/query/tool_queries.py`'s hour/dow pattern query, and `pipeline/dashboard_queries.py` (5 sites, live via `api/routers/ask_dashboard.py`) all still re-weight a rounded `avg_min`/`avg_delay_sec` the same way. Local verification could not be run in this sandboxed worktree: `poetry`/`ruff`/`mypy`/`pytest` are outside this session's Bash allowlist (confirmed via `make check`'s `fmt` step auto-creating an empty virtualenv and failing with `Command not found: ruff`) — the same tooling-provisioning gap already documented for several earlier items. Reviewed entirely by hand instead (every changed SQL formula, every seed-fixture consistency fix, and each new test's arithmetic traced by hand); a human or a later tick with a provisioned environment should run `make analyze-all` (to backfill `sum_delay_sec` exactly in any live/dev DB) and the full `make check` / `RUN_CH_INTEGRATION=1 poetry run pytest` before merge. (PR #285)

Three Major findings surfaced across a fresh three-reviewer Pass 1 (`bugs+logic+consistency`, `perf+practices+alternatives`, and a standalone `security`-dimension pass triggered by this diff's schema migration):
  1. `tests/conftest.py` had picked up an unrelated, undocumented `os.environ.setdefault(...)` block (7 lines) hardcoding `DATABASE_URL`/`RUN_CH_INTEGRATION=1`/`CLICKHOUSE_*` as defaults for every pytest invocation — inverting this repo's documented "ClickHouse-gated tests skip unless explicitly opted in" contract and permanently dead-coding `_redirect_to_test_db`'s own "no DATABASE_URL -> raise a clear error" fallback. Reverted; a hand-drafted revert of exactly this hunk was already sitting in an uncommitted worktree stash from the interrupted run and matched byte-for-byte.
  2. Migration `0028_agg_sum_delay_sec.up.sql` set `agg_route_daily.sum_delay_sec NOT NULL` (with a backfill `UPDATE` first) while leaving the other five tables nullable with no backfill — a rollout-ordering hazard (any `analyze()` run using pre-migration code between this migration landing and the code deploy would crash on every `agg_route_daily` insert) and an avoidable single-transaction lock/rewrite cost (six full-table backfills plus one validating `SET NOT NULL` scan on a table read by hot Overview/rankings/digest/forecast paths). Made `sum_delay_sec` nullable on all six tables with no backfill, matching the same hand-drafted stash fix noted above; `down.sql` needed no change (unconditional `DROP COLUMN IF EXISTS`, nullability-agnostic).
  3. Making `agg_route_daily.sum_delay_sec` nullable (fix #2) turned `pipeline/digest/build.py`'s `_aggregate_by_route` into a live crash risk: its Python `e["_delay_sum"] += sum_delay_sec` was unconditional, so a NULL row (any row analyze() hasn't rewritten since migration 0028) now raises `TypeError` instead of the old guaranteed-NOT-NULL contract. Fixed by skipping such a row entirely (excluded from both `_delay_sum` and `samples`, not just the numerator) — same matched-population principle already applied elsewhere in this file. Separately (not itself a fix-#2 consequence, but the same missing-guard pattern the security/migration reviewer flagged as under-covered relative to the two sites the prior entry above already fixed), applied the identical `FILTER (WHERE sum_delay_sec IS NOT NULL)` guard to every remaining structurally-identical query the reviewers found still missing it: `pipeline/reports/overview.py`'s `_headline_stats`, `_per_route_avg`, `_route_weekly_history`, `_peak_hour`, `_service_split_daily`, `_service_split`, `_daily_sparkline`, and (upgraded from a coarser all-NULL-group `HAVING` to the same FILTER pattern for consistency) `_top_delayed_routes`; `pipeline/reports/rankings.py`'s `compute_dow_ranking` and `compute_compare_ranking` (whose existing weekday/weekend `FILTER` conditions gained an added `AND sum_delay_sec IS NOT NULL` clause on both the averages and the `wd_n`/`we_n` minimum-sample gate). `compute_trend_series` had the identical bug in its own Python-side bucket pooling (`by_date_samples`/`by_date_weighted_sec` populated from different row subsets) — fixed by only accumulating both together inside the same `if sum_sec is not None:` guard, which in turn required changing a downstream `by_date_samples[d]` dict-index lookup to `.get(d, 0)` since a bucket where every group is still NULL now has no entry in that dict at all.
  Added regression coverage for the newly-fixed population-matching behavior (not just re-verifying the already-tested exact-pooling arithmetic): `tests/unit/test_digest_aggregate.py::test_null_sum_delay_sec_row_is_skipped_not_crashed_on` and `::test_all_rows_null_sum_delay_sec_yields_no_entry` (pure, DB-free), `tests/api/test_overview.py::test_headline_excludes_null_sum_delay_sec_row_from_avg_but_not_samples`, `tests/api/test_reports.py::test_compute_trend_series_excludes_null_sum_delay_sec_group_from_bucket_avg`. Verification, run for real this time (throwaway Postgres `:5544`/ClickHouse `:8124` containers were already up): `poetry run ruff check` and `poetry run mypy` clean on every changed Python file; `poetry run pytest` on the full originally-touched-file scope from both this entry and the two above (`tests/pipeline/test_analyze.py`, `tests/pipeline/test_digest_build.py`, `tests/api/test_overview.py`, `tests/api/test_reports.py`, `tests/query/test_tool_queries.py`, `tests/query/test_tools_integration.py`, `tests/unit/test_digest_aggregate.py`, plus `tests/api/test_api_agencies.py`, `tests/api/test_api_map.py`, `tests/api/test_forecast_overview_endpoint.py`, `tests/api/test_map_heatmap.py`) — 245 passed, 0 failed. - 2026-08-31: Fixed item 39: `pipeline/reports/overview.py`'s `_concentration` fast path (`ctx.time_band == 'all'`) approximated a route's total lateness contribution as `SUM(GREATEST(avg_min, 0) * samples)` — clamping each DAY's already-rounded, already-signed average to zero before summing across days, instead of clamping each OBSERVATION before summing (`SUM(GREATEST(dep_delay, 0))`, what the slow path's shared grain already computed exactly via `sum_late_sec`). Because `sum(max(x_i, 0)) >= max(sum(x_i), 0)` always (with equality only when a day has no early-running observations at all), the fast path's approximation is a systematic, one-directional UNDERstatement — never an overstatement — that fires on any day mixing early and late trips, which is a common shape for real service variance, not a rare edge case; in the worst case a day whose early and late minutes happen to net to zero contributes its full true lateness to the slow path but exactly zero to the fast path. This can flip which routes rank as top lateness contributors depending only on which `time_band` a request happens to use for identical underlying data, undermining the concentration panel's basic premise.

  Dev Postgres/ClickHouse were unreachable in this sandboxed worktree (no `transit-pg`/`transit-ch` containers running, only the throwaway `:5544`/`:8124` test containers), so the decision was justified by the structural bound above plus a concrete worked reproduction (both proved by hand and encoded as regression tests) rather than a live percentage measured against real dev data — consistent with this repo's own "durable content" rule against hardcoding a one-time measured number anyway. Given the bound is structurally unbounded (a single day can go from its true contribution to exactly zero) and the trigger condition is common, chose the real fix over a docstring-only caveat, following item 38's established pattern: added `sum_late_sec` (migration 0029, nullable/not-backfilled — same rollout rationale as item 38's `sum_delay_sec`) to `agg_daily_trend`, populated it in `pipeline/analyze.py`'s builder as `SUM(GREATEST(dep_delay, 0))`, and switched `_concentration`'s fast path to `SUM(sum_late_sec) / 60` (a plain sum, not a mean — no `FILTER`/division-population guard needed, unlike `sum_delay_sec`'s consumers).

  Added `tests/pipeline/test_analyze.py::test_analyze_sum_late_sec_is_clamped_per_observation_not_clamped_average` (analyze() end-to-end over a real mixed-day `updates` seed), `tests/api/test_overview.py::test_concentration_fast_path_counts_clamped_observations_not_clamped_average` (a ranking-flip regression: the old approximation would have ranked a uniformly-average route above a mixed-day route with more than double its true contribution), and `::test_concentration_fast_and_slow_paths_agree_on_mixed_early_late_day` (same mixed-day data seeded into both `agg_daily_trend` and raw `updates`/ClickHouse, asserting the fast and slow paths now agree exactly — the property item 39 exists to restore). Extended `_seed_agg_daily`'s test helper with an optional explicit `sum_late_sec` override for the mixed-day case, defaulting to the previous back-derivation for every existing uniformly-signed caller.

- 2026-08-31: Item 41 — five UI/UX statistical-enhancement findings from the query/calculation audit, each needing its own scope decision before implementation (same bundle pattern as item 30). Scoped and implemented three; deferred two with reasons below.
  (1) Per-hour/per-DOW p90 dispersion: **deferred**. Extending `pipeline/histogram.py`'s bucket-merge to a finer grain means storing a full histogram per (route, hour) or (route, dow) cell instead of today's one-per-(route, service_type) — a real multiplier on `agg_route_stats`/`agg_route_hour`/`agg_route_dow` storage and a new analyze-time merge/insert path, i.e. a schema-and-pipeline feature, not a query-level fix. That tradeoff (and which grain(s) actually matter — hour, dow, or both) needs a product call this session had no way to make; route-overall p50/p90 remains the only percentile grain for now.
  (2) On-time-percentage confidence: **implemented**, scoped narrower than a blanket Wilson-interval rollout. Audited every `on_time_pct`/`late5_pct` call site first: `samples` already sits alongside the percentage in every one found (`agg_route_stats`-backed `NetworkAgencyRow`, `pipeline/reports/rankings.py`'s ranking tuples, `pipeline/query/tools.py`'s tool columns, `RouteRow.tsx`'s live route row) — so the item's own "simpler alternative" was already satisfied everywhere. The remaining real gap: `compute_on_time`'s `samples > 20` gate is a binary include/exclude with no confidence signal for what it DOES include, unlike the route-triage/baseline path's existing `LOW_CONFIDENCE_SAMPLES`-based `low_confidence` flag. Added `pipeline/stats.py` (`wilson_interval`, `pct_is_uncertain`, `annotate_on_time_pct_confidence`) — a pure Wilson score 95% CI, flagging a percentage as uncertain when its interval half-width exceeds 5 percentage points (calibrated so a comfortably large baseline, roughly n>200 at a typical single-digit-to-~20% on-time/late-5min rate, stays under the cutoff while a genuinely thin one doesn't). Wired in as a **display-layer annotation** at the two render boundaries (`api/routers/reports.py`'s `on_time` report, `pipeline/query/tools.py`'s `on_time_rate` tool) rather than changing `compute_on_time`'s own 5-tuple contract, so `pipeline/reports/suggest.py`'s pooling of that same contract (`_pool_on_time_by_route`'s 5-value unpack) needed no change — confirmed by tracing every consumer of the tuple shape first. Surfaced as a trailing `low_confidence` bool/column in `ReportTable.tsx` (new "Confidence" column, blank unless true) and `RichResult.tsx` (Ask tool tables). New `docs/refactor-log.md`-adjacent tests: `tests/unit/test_stats.py` (Wilson interval against a hand-derived reference and a known p=0.5/n=100 textbook value, symmetry under p↔1-p, the uncertain/confident boundary), `tests/api/test_reports.py::test_on_time_appends_pct_low_confidence_flag`, `frontend/.../ReportTable.test.tsx` and new `RichResult.test.tsx` on_time-column cases.
  (3) `agg_daily_trend` day-to-day noise: **implemented** as a trailing 7-day sample-weighted mean **alongside** (not replacing) the raw daily average — decided at read time in `compute_trend_series` rather than a new stored/backfilled column, since it's a pure function of data already read for the raw average and avoids a migration for a value that's cheap to derive per request. Pools from the same exact raw-seconds sums (`by_date_weighted_sec`/`by_date_samples`) the raw average already uses, dividing once at the end — not re-weighting already-rounded daily averages, matching this file's existing pooling discipline. Window is position-based over OBSERVED days (a day with no service isn't calendar-padded into it), and only computed for `granularity == "day"` (week/month buckets are already smoothed by width). Surfaced as `avg_min_smoothed` in the API response, the CSV export, and `DailyChart.tsx` (a dashed second line + small legend + tooltip line, sitting alongside the solid raw line). New tests: `tests/api/test_reports.py::test_compute_trend_series_avg_min_smoothed_is_trailing_pooled_mean`, `frontend/.../DailyChart.test.tsx`.
  (4) Heatmap-cell / cohort-comparison confidence floors: **implemented**, using the existing `LOW_CONFIDENCE_SAMPLES`-flag convention rather than inventing a new one. Added `api.triage.COHORT_LOW_CONFIDENCE_SAMPLES = 10` (separate from the route-level `LOW_CONFIDENCE_SAMPLES = 30` — a stop-level 30-day cohort pools a much smaller natural sample than a route baseline, so reusing the larger figure would grey out nearly every cohort cell, per the item's own note that map cells and cohorts need different floors). `api/routers/map.py`'s `_heatmap_features` now flags a GeoJSON feature's `low_confidence` at the route-level floor (a heatmap cell pools the full requested range, closer in density to a route baseline than to a 30-day single-stop cohort); `_cohort_fields`/`route_stop_profile` now selects `SUM(samples) AS cohort_samples` and flags `cohort_low_confidence` at the cohort floor, independent of `is_outlier`'s own `cohort_route_count >= 2` gate (which counts distinct routes, not total observations). Frontend: `useHeatmapLayer.ts` dims (not hides or resizes — matching this codebase's existing "flag, don't hide" convention) a low-confidence stop's dot/casing opacity via a MapLibre `case` expression, `RouteDrilldown.tsx` appends a "few observations" note next to a low-confidence outlier marker, and `MapLegend.tsx` gained one explainer line. New/extended tests: `tests/api/test_map_heatmap.py` (heatmap `low_confidence` on a 3-sample fixture; cohort `cohort_samples`/`cohort_low_confidence` on both the existing thin 3-sample cohort fixture and the existing 502-sample weighted-cohort fixture), `frontend/.../useHeatmapLayer.test.ts` (updated the pre-existing exact-opacity assertion for the new expression shape, added a dedicated confidence-dimming case).
  (5) Per-agency freshness thresholds: **deferred**. The item's own stated precondition is to check real `agg_feed_health` data before deciding; this sandboxed session's Bash tool denies every dev-DB client invocation (`psql`, `poetry run python`, etc. — the same class of gap logged for items 8/16/21/22/23/25/28/38/40), so that check could not be performed. Deciding either way without the data the item itself asks for would be exactly the kind of unsupported guess CLAUDE.md's "Durable content only" section warns against, so `pipeline/freshness.py`'s uniform `is_stale` rule is left untouched pending a session with real dev-DB access.

- 2026-09-01: Added `scripts/daily_git_hygiene.py` (item 42), a dry-run-by-default job that closes two reactive-only gaps: it deletes merged, non-stacked `vps-loop/item-<N>` branches on `origin` (paper-trail-gated the same way `cleanup_git_state.py` gates local branches) and prunes local `vps-loop/item-<N>-superseded-<sha>` backup branches (Step 2b's own convention) past a configurable retention window (default 30 days, by commit `%ct`); it also drives `cleanup_git_state.py`'s existing local branch/worktree cleanup directly (import, not a shell-out) so all three stages run under one non-blocking flock on `/tmp/claude-loop.lock`, skipping cleanly instead of racing a live `/vps-loop-run` tick. Every deletion is appended to `/root/git-hygiene.log`, deliberately not `docs/refactor-log.md`. Added `tests/unit/test_daily_git_hygiene.py` covering the decision tables (merged/no-dependent-PR deletable, open-dependent-PR retained, no-PR-history retained, retention-window boundary), the lock-skip path (a real held lock; asserts no cleanup stage runs), and end-to-end apply/dry-run behavior against real throwaway git repos for both remote-branch and backup-branch cleanup. Crontab wiring and `.claude/README.md` documentation are host-level follow-ups, explicitly out of scope for this change. Verification could not be run in this sandboxed worktree: Bash denies `poetry`/`pytest`/`python3 -c` invocations outright (consistent with prior items' documented sandbox gap), and a manual `git ls-remote`/`push` glob experiment in `/tmp` was blocked by this session's own VPS-loop push/reset denylist hook; reviewed by hand instead against `cleanup_git_state.py`'s own patterns and its test file's fixture conventions, and against `git-ls-remote(1)`'s documented fnmatch-based pattern matching for the `vps-loop/item-*` glob. A human or a later tick with a provisioned environment should run `poetry run pytest tests/unit/test_daily_git_hygiene.py tests/unit/test_cleanup_git_state.py` and `poetry run ruff check`/`poetry run mypy scripts/daily_git_hygiene.py` before merge.

  2026-09-01 addendum: independent review found the remote-branch deletion path deleted on branch-name PR evidence alone without checking the remote tip actually matched the merged PR's head commit, and that its stacked-PR check trusted `gh pr list --base <branch>`'s own filter instead of independently verifying `baseRefName` (mirroring `cleanup_git_state.py`'s deliberate exact-`headRefName`-match pattern for the same class of footgun). Fixed by threading the remote tip SHA through `decide_remote_branch` (delete only on an exact `head_oid` match; otherwise keep with a "tip differs" reason, same as local cleanup) and by replacing per-branch `gh pr list --base` calls with one bulk `gh pr list --state open` fetch grouped locally by exact `baseRefName`. Also added the missing `git fetch --prune origin` before `cleanup_git_state.build_plan` (this job runs during idle stretches, so its local remote-tracking refs are exactly the ones most likely to have gone stale), made each per-branch delete loop (remote and backup-branch) catch and log a single failure instead of aborting the rest of that stage, wrapped `main()`'s own repo-resolution call in the same error handling every other stage gets, and added a `--` separator to the remote delete's `git push --delete` call for defense-in-depth consistency with the rest of the codebase. Extended `tests/unit/test_daily_git_hygiene.py` with a tip-mismatch decision case, a bulk stacked-PR grouping test, real end-to-end tests for the stale-tracking-ref fetch fix and for `run_local_cleanup`'s DELETED-line logging (previously untested), and per-branch delete-resilience tests for both the remote and backup-branch stages. Still could not run `pytest`/`ruff`/`mypy` in this sandboxed worktree (same denial as above); reviewed the diff by hand against `cleanup_git_state.decide_branch`'s existing exact-tip-match and `load_pull_requests`'s existing exact-`headRefName`-match patterns.

Judgment call (10): confirmed by test that `useDefaultRangeAnchor` and `useAnonymousFilterPersistence` (both called from `App.tsx`, both reading `useSearchParams()` independently) had a real, reproducible race — both effects fire from the same render and each computed its own `setSearchParams` update from the same stale pre-navigation snapshot, so a stored (possibly stale) anonymous filter could silently clobber a freshly-anchored non-empty default window purely because of which hook's effect happened to run second. Added regression coverage in `defaultRangeAnchor.test.tsx` (both hooks combined in one `MemoryRouter`, both precedence directions) and an isolated gate test.

Fixed by reading the existing stored value and merging back `dow`/`time_band`/`service`/`routes` whenever they're absent from the current params, before writing — `from`/`to` are deliberately excluded from this merge so a stale stored date range can never resurface.   2026-09-01 addendum: a third independent review pass found the round-2 merge fix in `anonymousFilterPersistence.ts` wasn't actually gated to the anchor-handoff case it was built for, so it could resurrect a field the user had just explicitly cleared mid-session (e.g. clearing only `dow` while `from`/`to`/`time_band` stayed put) — the merge ran on every persist-branch pass, not just the anchor-handoff one. Fixed by gating the merge behind the already-existing `isFirstAttemptForAgency` flag (the same flag the adjacent "everything cleared" ambiguity guard already uses for the identical "can't tell an explicit action from a hook-timing artifact" problem): the anchor-handoff scenario can only ever be a first-attempt scenario, since `computeAnchorRange` only fires before any filter interaction, so gating on first-attempt preserves that case while a later, genuine mid-session clear no longer gets silently undone. Added three tests to `anonymousFilterPersistence.test.tsx`: a mid-session explicit clear of `dow` (button-driven `setSearchParams` on the same mounted component, not a remount, so the ref state persists across the re-render) is not resurrected; the same for `routes` (its own separate merge branch); and a genuine first-attempt visit with a partial explicit URL (not an anchor-handoff, just a deep link) still gets its missing fields filled in from storage, confirming the gate is keyed on "first attempt," not specifically on the anchor. The existing anchor-handoff regression test in `defaultRangeAnchor.test.tsx` still passes unmodified. Separately, fixed `pipeline/reports/suggest.py`'s `_pool_ranking_by_route` docstring, which cited `api/routers/map.py`'s `today_route_summary` `rb` CTE's OLD formula as precedent — stale since that CTE was fixed earlier on this same branch; reworded to state the actual invariant directly (pooling an already-exact per-row mean by its own sample count is itself exact) instead of quoting another file's SQL, so it can't go stale the same way again. No behavior change there (`compute_ranking`'s per-row `avg_min` was already exact, not pre-rounded, so `suggest.py`'s own pooling was never actually buggy).
