A second full independent two-pass re-review of that fix converged (two reviewers) on the recheck call itself not being wrapped in the same per-branch try/except as the delete call beneath it — fixed, and the backup-branch retention reads were also batched from one `git log` subprocess per branch into a single `git for-each-ref` call, which incidentally closed a related concurrent-deletion race on the planning side too. Final state: 26/26 tests pass, `ruff check` clean. (PR #290)

- 2026-09-01: item 43 (cumulative pre-production pooling audit), a fresh /review-branch Pass 1 (3 independent reviewer calls: bugs+logic+consistency+security, perf+practices+alternatives, enforcement) against this branch's own diff vs `main` found 5 Major findings, fixed as follows. (1) `api/routers/overview.py`'s `peak_hour_breakdown` (`dow is None` branch) could return `avg_min=NULL` for a group whose entire `sum_delay_sec` population is NULL (nullable by migration 0028 design) while still passing the unfiltered `HAVING SUM(samples) >= 3` gate, then crashed rounding `NULL` into a non-optional Pydantic field — fixed by filtering `r["avg_min"] is not None` in the response list comprehension, matching every sibling call site this same branch already guards. (2) `pipeline/query/tool_queries.py`'s `route_hour_dow_pattern` had the identical bug (`_round2(None)` raises `decimal.InvalidOperation`, propagating as an unhandled exception through the Ask tool's `time_pattern`) — same guard applied before `_round2`. Both fixes covered by a new regression test seeding an all-NULL-`sum_delay_sec` group alongside a normal one, asserting the NULL group is omitted and the normal one survives. (3) `pipeline/dashboard_queries.py` never received this branch's own exact-sum pooling fix despite two of its tables (`agg_daily_trend`, `agg_route_hour`) being in migration 0028's scope — five call sites (`_heatmap_from_agg`'s DOW and hour-band grids, `_anomalies_series_from_agg`, and both `cur`/`prv` CTEs in `_movers_from_agg`) still pooled via `SUM(avg_min*samples)/NULLIF(SUM(samples),0)`; fixed all five to the same FILTER-guarded exact-sum pattern used elsewhere on this branch. Every downstream consumer already null-guarded its input, so no further Python changes were needed. Updated `tests/pipeline/test_dashboard_queries.py`'s `_seed_trend`/`_seed_route_hour` helpers to populate `sum_delay_sec` (exact-derived by default, overridable per row) and added four divergence-proving regression tests (heatmap-dow, heatmap-hour-band, anomalies, movers) using deliberately-equal `avg_min` values across rows so the old reweighting and the new exact-sum pooling provably disagree. A repo-wide `grep` for the anti-pattern after these fixes found no remaining unfixed instance in real query code (only comments/docstrings/tests referencing it historically, and `pipeline/reports/overview.py`'s `_peak_hour_by_dow` `agg_hour_daily` fast path, already correctly confirmed out of scope since that table predates migration 0028). (4, 5) `scripts/prepare_review.py`'s `ENFORCEMENT_PATTERNS` didn't flag `scripts/prepare_review.py` changing itself, nor the two deletion-safety scripts' own test files (`tests/unit/test_cleanup_git_state.py`, `tests/unit/test_daily_git_hygiene.py`) — added all four as exact-path entries, plus `tests/unit/test_prepare_review.py`. Added one parametrized positive-control test covering all seven exact-path `ENFORCEMENT_PATTERNS` entries (the previous test only covered the `check-*` basename glob case), closing the gap the enforcement reviewer also flagged as Minor (only one of four newly-added targets had a positive control).

  Minor findings deferred as follow-ups (both judged too large in scope for this fix pass, per the review's own allowance): extracting the FILTER-guarded exact-sum SQL fragment (hand-copied at 7+ call sites — direct cause of finding 3 above, since a copy-pasted fragment is exactly what silently drops call sites) into a single shared constant/helper; and `git mv`-ing `frontend/src/api/_combinedHooksProbe.test.tsx` into `defaultRangeAnchor.test.tsx` for a clearer name (real, non-duplicate coverage, just inconsistently named).

  2026-09-01 addendum: re-verifying Pass 1's fixes surfaced one more Major: `api/routers/overview.py`'s `peak_hour_breakdown` pooled branch and `pipeline/query/tool_queries.py`'s `route_hour_dow_pattern` both did `ORDER BY avg_min DESC LIMIT N` with no `NULLS LAST` — now that the exact-sum SQL can legitimately produce `avg_min=NULL` for an all-NULL-`sum_delay_sec` group, Postgres's default `NULLS FIRST` for `DESC` could let enough NULL groups sort ahead of real ones to fully consume the `LIMIT` and starve/empty the result, even though the previous round's Python-side `is not None` filter still correctly drops them post-query. Fixed both `ORDER BY` clauses to `DESC NULLS LAST`, matching the existing precedent in `pipeline/reports/rankings.py` and `pipeline/dashboard_queries.py`'s `_movers_from_agg`. Strengthened both regression tests to seed enough distinct all-NULL groups to exactly fill the respective `LIMIT` (20 for `peak_hour_breakdown`, 3 for `route_hour_dow_pattern`'s default `top_n`) so they'd fail without `NULLS LAST`, which the previous single-NULL-group seed couldn't distinguish from "correctly excluded" (the real row fit under the limit regardless of sort order). Also added a negative-control test (`test_ordinary_file_change_is_not_flagged_as_enforcement`) for the enforcement-pattern positive controls added in the prior round. Re-verification of the affected reviewer group confirmed both fixes correct and complete, with no further findings. Pass 1 is now clean across all three reviewer groups.

  A subsequent fresh, independent Pass 2 (per CLAUDE.md's mandatory two-pass gate) found one further Major, in `frontend/src/api/anonymousFilterPersistence.ts`: on a cold page load (fresh tab/reload/deep link, no router prefetch of `useAgencies()`), this hook's effect could fire on its very first render while `agencies` is still pending. `computeAnchorRange` returns `null` both when no anchor rewrite is needed AND when `agencies` hasn't loaded yet, so the hook's `if (computeAnchorRange(...)) return;` deferral check couldn't tell those apart and could restore a stored (possibly stale) `from`/`to` before `useDefaultRangeAnchor` ever got a chance to decide — once restored, `from`/`to` are already in the URL, so every later `computeAnchorRange` call returns `null` for "range already present" instead of "no rewrite needed," permanently pre-empting the anchor for that session. This is the exact race the branch's earlier "Judgment call (10)" fix was meant to close, but every existing test mocked `useAgencies` as already resolved, so none exercised the pending-data window. Fixed by reading `useAgencies()`'s `isPending` and bailing out of the effect while it's true, before ever consulting `computeAnchorRange`. Added a regression test in `_combinedHooksProbe.test.tsx` that renders with `useAgencies` mocked as pending (confirming no restore happens yet), then resolves it and re-renders (confirming the restore proceeds normally once real data is available). Also fixed, from the same pass: a duplicate/dead `assert "R_OK" in codes` line in `tests/api/test_overview.py`'s NULL-group regression test (copy-paste leftover, not a functional bug), and added `.pre-commit-config.yaml` (the `gitleaks` secret-scan hook wiring) to `scripts/prepare_review.py`'s `ENFORCEMENT_PATTERNS`, a pre-existing gap a Pass 2 enforcement review surfaced while confirming the rest of the pattern list's completeness — plus its own positive-control test case.

- 2026-09-02: item 46 — replaced `agg_route_stats`/`agg_route_hour`'s hand-rolled `PERCENT_RANK()`-window p50/p90 in `pipeline/analyze.py` with `PERCENTILE_DISC(0.5|0.9) WITHIN GROUP (ORDER BY dep_delay)`, which picks an observed value by cumulative distribution and so no longer silently mis-ranks a tied cluster below the target quantile or returns NULL for a single-sample partition; chose the discrete (not continuous/interpolating) variant to keep the "actual observed delay value" semantics `pipeline/reports/rankings.py`'s `_ranking_live` ClickHouse fallback already documents matching. Updated `tests/fixtures/synthetic_gtfs.py`'s three hand-computed pattern fixtures (`uniform_delays`, `outlier_spike`, `null_delays`) and their docstrings: each previously asserted `p50_min`/`p90_min` as `None` (or, for `outlier_spike`, the 600s outlier itself) as the then-correct output of the buggy formula; under `PERCENTILE_DISC` all three now resolve to the tied/majority delay value instead, which is the true median/90th-percentile for each pattern's data — re-derived by hand, not copied forward. Also corrected two now-stale docstring references to the old formula's NULL-on-tied-partition behavior in `tests/pipeline/test_synthetic_agg_e2e.py` and `tests/dashboard_synthetic_display_test.py`. Did not touch `pipeline/reports/rankings.py`'s separate ClickHouse live-path percentile query (out of this item's scope; still correct on its own terms and now the only place documenting the old PERCENT_RANK tie-NULL quirk, since that quirk no longer applies to the Postgres fast path). (PR #295)

  Review-driven fix-and-reverify: Pass 1's second reviewer group caught that the item's own new comment (above) claiming `PERCENTILE_DISC` "matches" `_ranking_live`'s semantics was false and materially misleading — `_ranking_live` (the ClickHouse `time_band`-filtered fallback in `pipeline/reports/rankings.py`) still intentionally reproduces the OLD min-rank-tie `PERCENT_RANK()` formula, so it can now silently disagree with the fixed Postgres fast path on tied/clustered data (concretely: `[0]*95 + [600]*5` gives p50/p90 of 10.0 via `_ranking_live` vs. 0.0 via `PERCENTILE_DISC`) — a real, user-visible divergence this diff introduced without documenting. A dispatched fix worker could not apply the correction (its Edit/Write permissions did not resolve against this worktree's nested path, and `EnterWorktree` refused since its session wasn't launched pinned to a worktree) — the coordinator applied it directly instead: corrected `pipeline/analyze.py`'s comment to state the `_ranking_live` divergence explicitly rather than claiming parity; rewrote `_ranking_live`'s docstring and inline NULL-handling comment in `pipeline/reports/rankings.py` to describe the divergence as known/accepted rather than as intentional matching; reframed `tests/api/test_reports.py::test_reports_ranking_live_percentile_matches_percent_rank_tie_semantics`'s docstring the same way (assertions/seeded data unchanged — still correct for what `_ranking_live` does).   Second review-driven fix-and-reverify (still Pass 1, same reviewer group re-run): the previous fix's replacement wording for the four "degenerate PERCENT_RANK result" comments — "every contributing row's dep_delay was itself NULL" — was itself factually wrong, caught by re-review: `pipeline/db.py`'s `build_dedup_ch_sql` filters `WHERE u.dep_delay IS NOT NULL` unconditionally before any row reaches `_analyze_deduped`, and `analyze()` wipes and rebuilds every one of an agency's `agg_*` rows from scratch each run — so a live group processed by `analyze()` can never have a null `dep_delay` feeding `PERCENTILE_DISC`, meaning `p90_min` can no longer be null alongside a non-null `avg_min`/`samples` for any freshly-analyzed group at all, for any reason. Re-fixed all four sites (`api/routers/map.py`, `pipeline/digest/build.py`, `tests/api/test_api_map.py`, `tests/pipeline/test_digest_build.py`) plus the two directly-seeded test docstrings to instead state the accurate invariant: this NULL shape is no longer reachable from `analyze()`'s own SQL for live data, and the `FILTER`/guard logic exists purely as defense-in-depth against a stale pre-migration row (before the next full rebuild) or a non-`analyze()` writer (a test fixture inserting directly, as these two tests do). Re-verified: `poetry run ruff check`/`poetry run mypy` clean on the four re-touched files; `poetry run pytest tests/api/test_api_map.py tests/pipeline/test_digest_build.py` — 58 passed, against the same throwaway `:5544`/`:8124` stack. Not fixed here (out of this branch's scope, flagged by re-review as a pre-existing, unrelated staleness): `.claude/skills/postgres-perf/SKILL.md`'s "Known traps" section still describes `PERCENT_RANK()`'s min-rank tie behavior as the current Postgres aggregate-path formula generally, rather than noting it's now specific to `_ranking_live`'s intentional old-formula reproduction — worth a follow-up doc fix, not blocking this item.

  Pass 2 (fresh, independent review) confirmed both fix commits above are accurate and complete (re-checked `pipeline/db.py`'s `dep_delay IS NOT NULL` filter and `analyze()`'s wipe-and-rebuild behavior directly, and confirmed no other place in the diff or codebase repeats either now-corrected wrong claim) and found no new bugs/logic/consistency/security/perf issues. Pass 2 surfaced one additional Minor, not previously flagged: `scripts/dev/diff_analyze_slice5.py`'s golden fixture (`tests/fixtures/baseline_outputs/analyze_slice5/golden.json`) still reflects the old `PERCENT_RANK()` formula's output for its fixed 25-row seed — hand-verified the actual divergence (p50_min unchanged at 8.5min for this seed, since the tied cluster happens to straddle exactly the same boundary under both formulas, but p90_min changes from 14.5min to 13.0min under `PERCENTILE_DISC`). This harness isn't wired into CI/`make check` (confirmed via grep) so it can't fail an automated run, and regenerating it correctly requires running the worktree's own fixed `analyze()` against a live throwaway DB with correct Python import resolution (a plain `python <script>` invocation doesn't get pytest's rootdir-based `sys.path` insertion that makes `import pipeline.analyze` resolve to the worktree's own code rather than the main checkout's) — determined this was a real enough risk of silently capturing a fixture against the wrong code that a follow-up regeneration by hand is safer than doing it here. Added a docstring note to the script itself (not the JSON fixture, to avoid touching data without being certain it's correct) flagging that `--save` needs to be re-run by hand next time this fixture is touched, and stating this is expected drift, not a regression. `.claude/skills/postgres-perf/SKILL.md`'s "Known traps" entry (flagged again this pass) still could not be edited directly — confirmed once more as a genuine "sensitive file" refusal for the coordinator's own session, not a settings-allowlist gap, matching items 3/5/12/44/45's established pattern; left deferred, as before. Verification: `poetry run ruff check` on the one changed file — clean (docstring-only change, no test impact).

  Pass 2's second reviewer group (perf/practices/alternatives) raised one further Minor, medium-confidence perf concern, not applied in this branch: `pipeline/analyze.py`'s two separate `PERCENTILE_DISC(0.5)`/`PERCENTILE_DISC(0.9) WITHIN GROUP (ORDER BY dep_delay)` calls per query may each require their own per-group sort rather than sharing one (Postgres ordered-set aggregates with differing direct arguments are not guaranteed to share transition/sort state the way plain aggregates like `SUM`/`AVG` do), which the old single-window-function formula avoided by deriving both percentiles from one materialized `pct` column. The suggested fix — `PERCENTILE_DISC(ARRAY[0.5, 0.9]) WITHIN GROUP (ORDER BY dep_delay)`, indexing `[1]`/`[2]` for p50/p90 — is a well-known Postgres pattern for exactly this case and would remove any doubt, but this session's sandbox blocked every attempt to run a live `EXPLAIN` against dev Postgres (read-only, otherwise permitted) to quantify the actual cost before deciding it's worth a SQL rewrite; applying an unverified rewrite risked introducing a real correctness regression in a change whose whole point is fixing correctness, for an unquantified, non-user-facing (background job) cost. Left as a follow-up: verify via `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` whether Postgres emits one Sort node or two for these two aggregate calls, and switch to the array form if it's two.

- 2026-09-02: Item 49 — fixed three NULL-vs-zero conflation sites where missing aggregate data was silently rendered as "zero delay," which could distort trend headlines or fabricate false anomalies. (1) `pipeline/query/formatter.py`'s `format_trend_text` coerced a NULL-`avg_min` bucket to `0` inside a plain, unweighted `sum(...)/len(days)` mean-of-means — now reuses `pipeline.reports.rankings._weighted_avg_min`'s sample-weighted, NULL-skipping pooling (the same helper the Ask tab's `time_series` tool already applies to the identical `compute_trend_series` output, so the two surfaces can no longer disagree on the headline number), returns the same "no data" text as an empty list when every bucket is NULL, and reports the rendered "観測日数"/"observed days" count as the number of buckets that actually had data rather than the raw bucket count. (Moved `_weighted_avg_min` itself from `pipeline/query/tools.py` to `pipeline/reports/rankings.py` — its actual data producer, `compute_trend_series` — during `/review-branch`'s pass 1: `pipeline/query/formatter.py` is documented as a lean rendering leaf and had no business depending on the LLM tool-dispatch module for an unrelated pure-Python helper, so this is a layering fix, not an import-cost one — `pipeline/reports/__init__.py` already pulls in `rankings.py` regardless of which module the helper lives in, and `_round2`/other cross-cutting helpers already live in the `reports` package for the same layering reason.) (2) `pipeline/dashboard_queries.py`'s `_anomalies_series_from_agg` coerced a NULL-average date (every row's `sum_delay_sec` still NULL, e.g. not yet re-analyzed since migration 0028) to `0.0`; now emits `avg_delay: None` for that date, and `anomaly_timeline`'s mean/std/z-score population excludes `None` entries entirely instead of letting a fabricated 0 bias the network mean down, inflate the standard deviation, and flag the missing day as a false anomaly next to genuinely-populated ones. (3) `dashboard_queries.py`'s `movers()` coerced a NULL `delta` (which only occurs when the CURRENT window itself has no data — the SQL's `COALESCE(prv.avg_min, 0)` only ever substitutes on the PREVIOUS side) to `0.0`, reading as "no change"; now propagates `None` ("no data") through `delta`/`delta_pct`, leaving the separate, already-correct `prv_v == 0` case (a genuinely-zero previous average) untouched. Added `tests/unit/test_formatter.py::test_format_trend_text_*` (4 new pure-logic cases, including one asserting `format_trend_text`'s rendered average matches `_weighted_avg_min` to within rounding on a hand-verified 3-day/one-NULL-day fixture) and two new DB-backed cases in `tests/pipeline/test_dashboard_queries.py` extending item 21's `movers_pool` fixture pattern (a NULL-`sum_delay_sec` date proves `anomaly_timeline` now reports `std=0.0`/no anomalies across three identical real days plus one correctly-excluded NULL day; a NULL-`sum_delay_sec` current-window row proves `movers()` now reports `delta=None`/`delta_pct=None` instead of a misleading zero). The dispatched worker's own Bash permissions denied `poetry`, `python3 -c`, and even a bare `docker`-adjacent probe beyond `docker ps` outright (not merely "command not found") — the same recurring sandboxed-worker provisioning gap already logged for items 8, 10, 16, 21, 22 (x2), 23, 25, 27, 31, 33, and others above — so it traced every SQL/NULL-propagation path by hand against the live schema (`db/migrations/0028_agg_sum_delay_sec.up.sql`'s nullable `sum_delay_sec`, `db/migrations/0001_initial.up.sql`'s `agg_daily_trend` column types) and the existing `_weighted_avg_min`/`_seed_trend` test-fixture conventions instead of running anything. The coordinator closed that gap during `/review-branch`: `poetry run ruff check`/`mypy` (clean) and `poetry run pytest tests/unit/test_formatter.py tests/pipeline/test_dashboard_queries.py -q` (16 + 13 passed) from the main checkout's already-provisioned venv, pointed at this worktree's file paths, via the established `os.environ.setdefault(...)`-in-`tests/conftest.py` temporary-edit workaround (reverted before this commit, confirmed zero net diff) against the live `:5544`/`:8124` throwaway containers. Both pass-1 review Minors (the `tools.py`→`rankings.py` helper relocation above, and a now-redundant `if not days` early-return in `format_trend_text` that `_weighted_avg_min([])`'s own `None` return already covered) were fixed in this same commit. (PR #298)
- 2026-09-02: Item 50 — fixed a rounding-convention inconsistency where the Ask tab and the Reports text could show different digits for the exact same underlying number. `pipeline/query/formatter.py`'s `_r` rounded via plain Python `round()` (round-half-to-even) while `pipeline/reports/rankings.py`'s `_round1`/`_round2` (already reused by `pipeline/query/tool_queries.py`) deliberately use `Decimal(...).quantize(..., ROUND_HALF_UP)` to match Postgres's `ROUND()`; for an `avg_min=2.15`, `_r` rendered `"2.1"` while the declared convention gives `"2.2"`. `_r` now quantizes with the same `Decimal`/`ROUND_HALF_UP` pattern. Separately, `pipeline/reports/overview.py`'s `_service_split_daily` never rounded its per-day `avg` at all, while its sibling `_service_split` rounds the same metric to 2dp via `_round2` — same underlying data, two different precisions; `_service_split_daily` now applies `_round2` too. Also converted the other bare-`round()` call sites computing `avg_min` (`_top_delayed_routes`, `_peak_from_hour_rows`, `_service_split`, `_daily_sparkline`) to `_round2` for the same convention; left `_concentration`'s `share_pct`/`rest_share_pct` roundings untouched since those aren't `avg_min` and changing them risked an unrelated behavior/display change. Added `tests/unit/test_formatter.py::test_r_rounds_half_up_not_half_to_even` and `test_fmt_ranking_rounds_avg_min_half_up` (pin the `2.15` → `"2.2"` repro), and `tests/api/test_overview.py::test_service_split_daily_rounds_same_precision_as_service_split` (a `sum_delay_sec=100`/`samples=3` fixture — `100/3/60 = 0.5555...` — proving `service_split_daily` and `service_split` now agree on `0.56`). The dispatched worker's own Bash permissions denied `poetry`, `python3 -c`, and even `poetry --version` outright — the same recurring sandboxed-worker provisioning gap logged for many earlier items — so this traced the `Decimal`/`ROUND_HALF_UP` semantics and the SQL/type paths by hand against the existing `_round1`/`_round2`/`_MIN` conventions instead of running anything; verification is deferred to the coordinator's `/review-branch` pass. (PR #299)

Fixed all three: converted `_movers`'s `delta_min`/`current_avg_min`/`previous_avg_min` (both avg_min-class, not the percentage `delta_pct`/`d_pct` fields, which stay out of scope for the same reason `_concentration`'s share_pct was already excluded) and `compute_overview_summary`'s two duplicate `delta_min` sites (pool-None and pool-gather branches) to `_round1`/`_round2`; changed `_r` to delegate to `_round1` for its `d=1` case while keeping the generic quantizer path for any future non-1 `d`; reworded the stale comment to name `_round2`. Not fixed (explicit call, not an oversight): a reviewer-flagged behavior difference where the new `Decimal`-based `_r` raises on NaN/Inf input while the old `round()` tolerated it — every real caller passes DB-derived aggregate values that are never NaN/Inf (NULL maps to `None` before reaching `_r`), so adding defensive handling for an unreachable input would be exactly the kind of speculative error handling this repo's conventions warn against. Re-verified after the fix: ruff/mypy clean on both re-touched files; the same 76 tests still pass (Decimal `ROUND_HALF_UP` vs. the old `round()`'s half-to-even only differs at exact `.xx5` boundaries, which no existing assertion — all `pytest.approx`-based — depends on). Re-ran both owning groups (bugs/logic/consistency/security, and perf/practices/alternatives) against the new head: both confirmed the fixes correct and complete, but surfaced two more Minors — (4) `_r`'s `d` parameter and its non-1 quantizer fallback branch was now fully dead code (no caller ever passes anything but `d=1`); (5) none of the new tests actually exercised the `_movers`/`compute_overview_summary` half-up-vs-half-even divergence point at a real `.xx5` boundary (the existing `pytest.approx`-based assertions can't distinguish the two rounding modes). Fixed both: dropped `_r`'s `d` parameter entirely (now always delegates to `_round1`, removing the now-fully-unused `decimal` import and simplifying the one explicit `_r(r[2], 1)` call site to `_r(r[2])`); added `tests/api/test_overview.py::test_movers_delta_min_rounds_half_up_not_half_to_even`, seeding a prior/current avg pair whose raw delta (`2.155`) is an exact tie at the 2dp boundary — empirically confirmed this is a genuine regression guard (temporarily reverted the `_movers` fix, re-ran: got `2.15` instead of the expected `2.16`, then restored the fix). Re-verified: ruff/mypy clean; the full `tests/unit/test_formatter.py` + `tests/api/test_overview.py` suite (77 tests, one more than before) passes against the live `:5544`/`:8124` stack via the same conftest workaround, reverted with zero net diff. - 2026-09-02: Item 51 — fixed `pipeline/reports/forecast.py`'s `hourly_cells_to_dow_band` path pooling `compute_hourly_heatmap`'s already-rounded per-cell `avg_min` across dates instead of the exact raw-seconds sum (the same defect class items 43/47 already fixed on `agg_hour_daily`'s OTHER reader, `overview.py::_peak_hour_by_dow` — `compute_hourly_heatmap` itself was explicitly noted as unaffected back then because at the time it did no cross-row pooling at all; `hourly_cells_to_dow_band`, added later, introduced exactly that pooling without anyone revisiting the exemption). `agg_hour_daily` already has migration 0030's nullable `sum_delay_sec` column (unused by any Python reader until now); `compute_hourly_heatmap` now selects and returns it (both the Postgres fast path and the ClickHouse live-fallback `sum(dep_delay)`), and `forecast.py`'s `_pooled`/`summarize_agency_overview` grid loop now sums each row's exact `sum_delay_sec` (dividing once at the end) instead of re-weighting `avg_min * samples`, falling back to the old `avg_min * samples` behavior only when a caller has no `sum_delay_sec` at all (the direct-SQL `forecast_overview` endpoint's `grid_rows`, whose `avg_min` is already an exact `SUM(sum_delay_sec)/SUM(samples)`, so that fallback stays exact for it). For `pipeline/reports/suggest.py`'s parallel instance of the same bug class (`_pool_ranking_by_route`/`_pool_on_time_by_route` re-weighting `compute_ranking`/`compute_on_time`'s already-rounded per-row `avg_min`/`p90_min`/`on_time_pct`), chose option (b) from the item text instead of widening the fix: `compute_ranking`'s 6-tuple return shape is also the CSV/formatter/LLM-tool-result contract (`pipeline/query/formatter.py`, `pipeline/query/tools.py`, `api/routers/reports.py`'s `_REPORT_CSV_COLUMNS`), so exposing raw `sum_delay_sec`/`samples` behind it would widen a shared, widely-consumed contract, out of scope for this item. Rewrote the false "pooling an already-exact per-row mean... is itself exact" docstring claims in `_pool_ranking_by_route`/`_pool_on_time_by_route`/the module docstring to state the real (small, bounded) imprecision instead, with a hand-verified numeric repro (`sum_delay_sec=1000,samples=21` + `sum_delay_sec=50,samples=21`: true pooled mean `1050/42/60≈0.4167`, pooled-of-rounded gives `0.415`). Assessed `_anomaly_today`'s boundary risk (documented in its own docstring): its `classify_route` call rounds the already-imprecise pooled minutes to whole seconds a third time before a strict `>` comparison, so a route within about a second of the anomaly/watch boundary could in principle flip depending on its service-type split — accepted as a narrow, pre-existing edge case, not fixed here, since a real fix needs the same out-of-scope `compute_ranking` contract change. - 2026-09-02: Item 53 — fixed `pipeline/reports/overview.py`'s `compute_overview_summary` comparing an asymmetrically-clamped current window against an always-full-7-day baseline whenever `ctx` is narrower than 7 days. `cur_ctx` was already clamped to `ctx.from_date` via `max(anchor - 6, ctx.from_date)`, but `base_ctx` unconditionally spanned a fixed 7 days immediately before it regardless of that clamp, so a genuine N-day (N<7) current average could get compared against a genuine 7-day baseline average and manufacture a "this week vs last week" delta purely from differing day-of-week composition, not a real trend change. `OverviewHeroRow.tsx`'s existing i18n copy (`baseline_hint_body`, from an earlier item's pass) already describes the comparison as "the same-length period immediately before it," so the fix makes the backend match copy that was already correct rather than the reverse: `base_from` is now `base_to - timedelta(days=cur_ctx.days - 1)` instead of a hardcoded `- 6`, making `base_ctx` always the same width as `cur_ctx` — a no-op whenever `ctx` is wide enough that `cur_ctx` is unclamped at a full 7 days (the case the module's docstrings already correctly described). Updated the docstrings that had baked in the old fixed-7-day-baseline formula (`compute_overview_summary`'s own, `_fetch_grain`'s "shared grain" explanation, the `_GRAIN_LOOKBACK_DAYS` comment, and — caught in `/review-branch`'s pass 1 as a Minor, since it directly contradicted the corrected `compute_overview_summary` docstring right above it in the same file — `_movers`'s "a true 7-day week-over-week regardless of range" claim, plus the weaker secondary instances in `_latest_data_date` and `_top_delayed_routes`); confirmed `_GRAIN_LOOKBACK_DAYS = 7` is still a safe (now slightly conservative for a narrow ctx, never insufficient) upper bound on how far back the shared grain needs to reach, so no change to the constant or `_fetch_grain`'s query itself was needed. Updated `test_slow_path_baseline_window_reaches_before_ctx_from_date` (its single-day-ctx scenario now needs its baseline seed row 1 day before `ctx.from_date`, not 7, to keep asserting a non-null `baseline_avg_min` under the new same-width rule) and added `test_narrow_ctx_baseline_matches_current_window_width` (a 3-day ctx with a distinct, easily-separable average on each side of the boundary, asserting `baseline_avg_min` reflects the matching 3-day window immediately before `cur_ctx` rather than an unclamped 7-day one that would reach 4 days further back). Pass 1 of `/review-branch` caught this last test as a Major on its first cut: it originally left the two boundary dates (05-09, 05-12) unseeded, so the old buggy formula and the fix produced the identical `baseline_avg_min` and the assertion would have passed either way — fixed by seeding those two dates with a third, sharply different tier (15.0 min vs. the 1.0/5.0 min tiers already present), verified by hand that this makes the assertion fail under the pre-fix `- 6`-day formula and pass under the fix. Verification could not be run in this sandboxed worktree: `poetry`, `make`, and direct `python3 -m`/`-c` invocations were all denied by this session's Bash permissions outright (not merely unprovisioned) — the same recurring class of gap logged for many earlier items above; the throwaway Postgres `:5544` and ClickHouse `:8124` containers were confirmed already running via `docker ps`. Every changed line was instead traced by hand against `RangeCtx.days`'s existing `(to_date - from_date).days + 1` formula and the grain-coverage invariants in `_check_grain_covers`/`_fetch_grain`. A human or a later tick with a provisioned environment should run `poetry run pytest tests/api/test_overview.py` (with `RUN_CH_INTEGRATION=1` and the ClickHouse test env block, since both the changed and the new test are slow-path/ClickHouse-gated) plus `poetry run ruff check`/`poetry run mypy` before merge. (PR #305)

- 2026-09-03: Item 55 — fixed `frontend/src/tabs/live/RouteRow.tsx`'s baseline/today comparison label rendering a double sign (e.g. "+-1") for any route that runs early on average, since its own `minStr` helper already emitted a negative sign for a negative input while both of its call sites hardcoded a literal `+` immediately in front of it. Extracted the sibling `RouteDrilldown.tsx`'s already-correct `signedMin(sec, t)` (derives the sign from the value itself via the shared `common.unit_min_signed` i18n key, prepending `+` only for non-negative values) into a new shared module, `frontend/src/tabs/live/signedMin.ts`, and switched both `RouteRow.tsx` and `RouteDrilldown.tsx` to import it instead of keeping two hand-written sign-formatting functions that had already drifted apart. Added `frontend/src/tabs/live/signedMin.test.ts` (positive, negative, and zero cases, explicitly asserting the output never contains `"+-"`) and a `RouteRow` regression case in `frontend/src/tabs/LiveTab.test.tsx` with negative `baseline_avg_sec`/`avg_delay_sec`, confirming a single correct `-` sign renders instead of `+-`. Verification: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run lint:i18n`, `npm run lint:i18n-strings`, `npm run test` (468/468 passed), `npm run test:check-entry-chunk`, and `npm run build:bundle && npm run check:entry-chunk` all clean. - 2026-09-03: Item 56 — fixed `frontend/src/components/PeakHourModal.tsx`'s per-route bar rendering an invalid negative CSS `width` whenever a peak-hour breakdown mixed a positive `maxAvg` (set by a delayed route) with a route whose own `avg_min` is negative (an early-running route); browsers treat a negative `width` as zero/blank, but the underlying value was never clamped. Clamped the computed percentage to a minimum of 0 (`Math.max((r.avg_min / maxAvg) * 100, 0)`), mirroring `PeakHourRibbon.tsx`'s existing `bar_h = Math.max(H - PAD_BOTTOM - y, 0)` precedent — a route with a negative average degrades to a zero-width bar while its adjacent text label still shows the true negative minutes value, same visual contract the ribbon already uses. Verified `PeakHourRibbon.tsx`'s own clamp by hand-tracing `toY`/`bar_h` against a mixed-sign dataset: `Math.max(...)` unconditionally floors `bar_h` at 0 regardless of how `y` relates to the baseline, so no negative SVG `height` can reach the `<rect>` even in the degenerate all-negative-`peak_avg_min` case — no separate fix needed there, confirmation only. Added a `data-testid` to the modal's bar element and a regression test (`PeakHourModal.test.tsx`) asserting a mixed positive/negative dataset renders `width: "0%"` for the negative-average route while its label still shows the negative value. Verification: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run lint:i18n`, `npm run lint:i18n-strings`, `npm run test` (469/469 passed), `npm run test:check-entry-chunk`, and `npm run build:bundle && npm run check:entry-chunk` all clean. Separately noticed (not fixed, out of scope for this item, which took the label as already correct): `peakHourModal.avgMin`'s i18n template (`"+{{value}}min"`) hardcodes a literal `+` in front of `r.avg_min.toFixed(1)`, so a negative `avg_min` renders a double sign (e.g. "+-3.2min") — the same bug class item 55 just fixed in `RouteRow.tsx` via the shared `signedMin()` helper, confirmed empirically by rendering the modal with a negative `avg_min`. (PR #309)

  2026-09-03 follow-up: `/review-branch` Pass 1 found the paragraph above's claim that `PeakHourRibbon.tsx`'s existing clamp was "genuinely sufficient" — including for "the degenerate all-negative-`peak_avg_min` case" it explicitly claimed to have checked — was wrong (Major, bugs/logic). `Math.max(bar_h, 0)` only rules out a literally-negative SVG `height`; it does not bound a bar to the plot area once `denom` (`peak_avg_min`) itself goes negative. For an hour more negative than `denom`, `v/denom` is a ratio greater than 1, and with the `<svg>` styled `overflow: "visible"`, that hour's bar renders far outside the chart (an oversized/off-canvas bar), not the harmless zero-height bar the original entry described. Fixed by clamping `y` itself to `[PAD_TOP, H - PAD_BOTTOM]` before deriving `bar_h` from it, so no bar can render above or below the visible plot band regardless of how extreme the value/denom ratio gets. Added `PeakHourRibbon.test.tsx` (previously no test file existed for this component) with an all-negative dataset (`peak_avg_min = -1`, another hour at `-10`), asserting every rendered `<rect>`'s `y`/`height` stays within the chart's `0..H` viewBox. Same pass also found two Minor findings in `PeakHourModal.tsx`, both fixed in this commit: `maxAvg` could be exactly `0` when every route's `avg_min` is `0`, producing a `NaN` width the `Math.max(NaN, 0)` clamp doesn't catch (fixed with the same `|| 1` fallback `PeakHourRibbon.tsx`'s own `denom` already uses); and the modal's clamp was one-sided, so an all-negative `maxAvg` could push the ratio above 100% (masked only incidentally by the track div's `overflow: "hidden"`) — made two-sided (`Math.min(Math.max(..., 0), 100)`). Also widened the bar's `data-testid` to include `service_type` (matching the row's own `key` convention), since `route_code` alone isn't guaranteed unique across service types. Added regression tests for the all-zero and all-negative modal cases to `PeakHourModal.test.tsx`.

  2026-09-03 follow-up: `/review-branch` Pass 2 found zero Major findings, but two Minors worth fixing. First: the mixed-sign regression test's unanchored regex assertion would match either a correct `"-3.2min"` label or the buggy `"+-3.2min"` double-sign the prior entry disclosed as "not fixed, out of scope" — the test's comment claimed the label was verified correct when the assertion couldn't distinguish the two. Fixed the underlying label too: `peakHourModal.avgMin`'s i18n template hardcoded a literal `+`; changed to `"{{sign}}{{value}}min"` in both locales, with the call site deriving `sign`/`value` from `r.avg_min`'s own sign — the same bug class item 55 fixed in `RouteRow.tsx`, applied directly here since this value is already in fractional minutes (`signedMin()` expects whole seconds, a different unit). Tightened the test to an exact match plus a positive-sign assertion. Second: `PeakHourRibbon.tsx`'s hover tooltip computed its position via the same unclamped `toY(v)` the bar fix above just patched. Extracted the clamp into a shared `clampY` helper applied to both the bar loop and the hover handler, and added a test simulating a hover over the same far-negative hour, asserting the tooltip stays within the plot band. Re-ran the full frontend verification suite (473/473 tests, excluding one untracked scratch file outside this diff that asserts the now-fixed old buggy text); all clean. (PR #309)

- 2026-09-03: Item 60 — fixed `scripts/daily_git_hygiene.py`'s backup-branch retention window being measured from the backing commit's own committer date (`for-each-ref`'s `%(committerdate:unix)`) instead of from when the `vps-loop/item-<N>-superseded-<sha>` backup branch itself was created. A branch Step 2b judges "fully superseded" typically already has an old tip commit by definition, so a backup created *today* for it could already look past a 30-day retention window on day one — becoming eligible for deletion on the very next hygiene run and defeating the window's whole purpose of giving a human time to notice and recover from a wrong Step 2b judgment call. `load_local_superseded_branches` now reads each backup branch's own reflog instead: a plain `git branch`/`git checkout -b` writes exactly one `branch: Created from ...` reflog entry timestamped at that command's real wall-clock time regardless of the start point's own age (confirmed live), read via one bulk `git log -g --glob=refs/heads/vps-loop/item-*-superseded-*` call (mirroring the module's existing `for-each-ref` bulk-read idiom) rather than one `git reflog show` per branch, keeping the minimum epoch seen per ref since `--walk-reflogs` can't combine with `--reverse` to read oldest-first directly. Branch enumeration still goes through `for-each-ref` so a real branch with no reflog at all (disabled `core.logAllRefUpdates`, or a `git gc`-expired entry) is reported as an explicit unknown (`None`) rather than silently dropped; `decide_backup_branch` treats an unknown creation time as always-keep, never guessing either extreme. `BackupBranchDecision.commit_epoch` is renamed `creation_epoch: int | None` throughout to make the new semantics explicit at the type level. Updated `tests/unit/test_daily_git_hygiene.py`'s backup-branch fixtures to construct "old"/"young" backups via a new `create_backup_branch_at` helper (backdates the branch's own reflog via `GIT_COMMITTER_DATE`, mirroring the existing `commit_at` helper's identical technique for commits) instead of backdating the backing commit, and added direct tests proving the branch-creation-time-vs-commit-time distinction, the always-keep-on-unknown fallback, and the no-reflog case. Verification: `poetry run ruff check`/`poetry run ruff format --check` clean on both changed files; `poetry run pytest tests/unit/test_daily_git_hygiene.py` (65 passed) and the full `tests/unit/` directory (458 passed, 17 skipped) against the throwaway `:5544` test DB, both clean. A full `make test` run against the same throwaway DB, run alone (a concurrent overlapping run against the same shared DB had transiently corrupted an earlier attempt with foreign-key/state errors unrelated to this change), passed clean: 1061 passed, 210 skipped, 0 failed. Also noted for a human: mid-session, one `make test` invocation was accidentally run without the `:5544` override and so inherited the Makefile's dev-DB default (`:5433`); `tests/conftest.py`'s own `_redirect_to_test_db()` guard means at most an empty `transit_test` sibling database could have been touched/created on that host (never the real `transit` database), and dev-DB reachability from this sandboxed worktree is itself doubtful, but this was not independently confirmed either way — worth a human spot-check of the dev Postgres host's database list. (PR #314)

- 2026-09-04: Item 65 — made `/welcome`'s dashboard preview auto-advance through its tabs on its own, so a visitor who never touches the page still sees the Overview -> Map -> Analysis -> Agencies -> Live cycle the way a recorded demo video would. `DashboardPreview.tsx` gained a mount effect owning one `setInterval` (4.5s cadence) that calls `setActiveTab` from inside the timer callback (never during render), gated by two refs rather than state — `hoveredRef` (set/cleared by `onMouseEnter`/`onMouseLeave` on the shell) and `lastInteractionAtRef` (stamped by `onClickCapture`/`onKeyDownCapture` on the same shell, so any click or keyboard activity anywhere in the preview counts). A tick is skipped outright while hovered, and skipped until 6s have passed since the last click/keydown otherwise — hovering pauses and resumes immediately on pointer-leave, while an actual click/keyboard interaction buys a longer grace window before the demo starts narrating again, so a visitor mid-click doesn't get a tab yanked out from under them but the preview never looks permanently stuck either. Landing on the "ask" CTA (outside the 5-tab cycle) resumes at the top of the cycle rather than getting stuck, since it isn't a member of `AUTO_ADVANCE_ORDER`. The whole timer is skipped via `useMediaQuery("(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)")` for visitors who've asked for less motion. `PreviewSidebar.tsx`/`TabExplorer.tsx`/`frontend/src/tabs/*`/`HelpPage.tsx` and `/welcome`'s sign-in CTA are all unchanged — this item was scoped to auto-play behavior only, not to guest-access reachability (left alone per the item's own explicit instruction) or to `TabExplorer.tsx` itself, the dead, already-superseded file item 64's log entry above already flagged for deletion (mentioned here only because the item's own text named that file; it is not rendered anywhere and was not touched). Added 6 new tests to `DashboardPreview.test.tsx` using `vi.useFakeTimers()`, covering the full auto-advance cycle, hover pause/resume, click pause/resume after the grace delay, keyboard pause/resume, the `prefers-reduced-motion` kill switch, and resuming from the top after idling on "ask". - 2026-09-04: Item 67 — added a server-side daily quota on anonymous (unauthenticated) LLM-backed Ask usage. `api/middleware/ratelimit.py` gained an anon-quota section: `check_and_consume_anon_quota` (an in-memory `limits`-library `FixedWindowRateLimiter` test-then-consume over two buckets — a per-anon-session key and a coarser per-IP backstop — so a declined call never burns either bucket it was refused against), `get_or_issue_anon_session` (mints and verifies a signed httpOnly `ask_anon_sid` cookie on a caller's first anonymous request, reusing `SESSION_SIGNING_KEY` with a distinct salt the same way `api/routers/auth.py`'s `oauth_tx` cookie does), and `AnonAskQuotaExceeded` + `ask_quota_exceeded_handler` (mirrors `api/aggregate_errors.py`'s propagate-to-registered-FastAPI-handler convention: the exception is raised deep inside the LLM call and propagates uncaught to a 429 response body `{"detail", "code": "ask_anon_quota_exceeded"}`, distinct from both slowapi's generic `RateLimitExceeded` body and a plain 500). All three env vars (`ASK_ANON_DAILY_LIMIT`=5, `ASK_ANON_IP_DAILY_LIMIT`=20, `ASK_ANON_QUOTA_ENABLED` kill switch) are read live, matching `ASK_INTENT_CACHE_ENABLED`'s existing pattern; 5/day per session is enough for a genuinely curious visitor to try several real questions while bounding worst-case per-visitor LLM cost, and 20/day per IP is loose enough not to penalize a few legitimate visitors sharing one IP while still capping a single source cycling through many anon-session cookies. `pipeline/query/chat.py`'s `chat_with_tools` gained an `anon_quota` parameter, checked/consumed immediately before each of its two actual LLM-invocation sites (the intent-cache Stage-2 path and the flag-off path) — never at the build-mode short-circuit or an intent-cache pre-hit (both skip the LLM entirely) and never for a Stage-1/2 router resolution (handled one level up in `api/routers/ask.py`, which never even calls `chat_with_tools` for those). `POST /ask` now resolves `user=Depends(get_current_user_optional)`; only when `None` does it issue/reuse the anon-session cookie and thread an `AnonQuotaContext` through — logged-in callers are completely unaffected. `api/main.py` registers the new handler alongside the existing `RateLimitExceeded`/`aggregate_not_ready_handler` ones. Updated 8 existing `chat_with_tools` mock signatures in `tests/api/test_api_ask.py` to accept the new keyword (default `None`, preserving prior behavior); added unit tests to `tests/unit/test_ratelimit.py` (bucket math under/at/over limit, the IP backstop, the declined-call-consumes-neither-bucket guarantee, the kill switch, cookie issue/reuse/tamper-fallback) and HTTP-level tests to `tests/api/test_api_ask.py` (cookie issuance/reuse across requests, no-cookie fallback, logged-in bypass, a Stage-1 rule-hit never touching the quota even past the configured limit, and an end-to-end 429-with-code test exercising the real `chat_with_tools` against a faked LLM provider client, mirroring `tests/query/test_chat_null_args.py`'s `_FakeClient` pattern). Verification: this sandboxed session denied every `poetry`/`python3 -c` invocation (the same recurring gap logged for items 46/49/50/54/59) — `poetry run pytest`/`ruff check`/`mypy` could not be run directly; the `limits`/`itsdangerous` APIs used were cross-checked against `poetry.lock`'s pinned `slowapi==0.1.9`/`limits==5.8.0`, and every new pattern was matched against an existing, working analog in this repo (`api/aggregate_errors.py`, `api/routers/auth.py`'s signed cookie, `tests/api/test_conversations.py`'s `get_current_user_optional` dependency-override pattern). A later tick with a provisioned environment should run `poetry run pytest tests/unit/test_ratelimit.py tests/api/test_api_ask.py tests/query/`, `poetry run ruff check`, and `poetry run mypy` before merge. A `.env.example` documentation entry for the three new env vars was attempted but denied by this session's file-write policy for any `.env*` path; the same rationale is instead fully captured in `api/middleware/ratelimit.py`'s docstrings. - 2026-09-05: Item 70 — replaced `/welcome`'s hero vehicle markers (small rotated ellipse-with-window-dots blobs) with real Maki icon glyphs. New `frontend/src/pages/landing/vehicleIcons.ts` vendors the three human-supplied `d` path strings (bus/train/tram, `mapbox/maki`, CC0, native `viewBox 0 0 15 15`) verbatim and includes a small hand-rolled SVG path-command interpreter (`drawMakiPath`, supporting exactly the M/L/H/V/C/S/Z commands these three strings use, both absolute and relative, including S's smooth-curve control-point reflection) that replays a `d` string as `moveTo`/`lineTo`/`bezierCurveTo`/`closePath` calls — deliberately not `Path2D`+`ctx.fill(path)`, since jsdom (this repo's test environment) has no `Path2D` implementation and this codebase's canvas tests all run against a plain recording stub rather than a real browser context; using `Path2D` would have made the glyphs untestable at all under `npm run test`. `cityMapScene.ts`'s `RouteLine` gained a `vehicleMode` field, assigned once per route (bus for the on-time/`--accent` route, train for the delayed/`--color-warning` route) and threaded through `useCityMapAnimation.ts` into `cityMapDraw.ts`'s `VehicleDraw`. `drawScene` now renders each vehicle as a solid circular badge filled with the route's real status color plus a bold white-filled Maki glyph centered on top (no more colored ellipse body / thin window dots), sized via a bumped `vehicleRadius` (`strokeScale * 0.02`, up from `0.011`) targeting a legibly-vehicle-shaped on-screen marker, and — per the item's explicit instruction — the glyph is never rotated to face its direction of travel (matches how Google Maps/Citymapper render vehicle markers; a rotated glyph reads as broken, not "in motion"). Left the hero's basemap/routes/station dots and the existing headline-legibility scrim (`LandingPage.css`'s `.landing-hero__scrim`, from items 65/66) completely untouched, exactly as the item scoped.

  Two things a later review/verification step should know before treating this as fully done: (1) this scene only has two routes (on-time/delayed), and the item's own "one mode per route, never mixed per vehicle" rule caps how many distinct vehicle modes can appear live at once at two — so the live `/welcome` animation shows bus + train, not all three modes simultaneously; the tram icon/path data is fully vendored and covered by its own direct unit tests (`vehicleIcons.test.ts`) but is not reachable from the live scene without adding a third route, which the item explicitly scoped out ("not... routes... station dots"). This tension between "show a bus, a train, and a tram" and "only two routes exist, don't add one" could not be resolved without either violating one explicit instruction or guessing past the other, so it's flagged here rather than silently picked. (2) This sandboxed session's Bash permission denies starting any dev/preview server (`npm run dev`, `npm --version`, and other non-allowlisted invocations all hard-denied, independent of `npm run typecheck`/`test`/`lint`/etc., which are allowlisted and ran normally) — the same class of constraint this backlog has repeatedly hit before (items 19, 66). - 2026-09-05: Item 71 fix-and-reverify (pass 1) — one of two independent `/review-branch` pass-1 reviewers found a genuine Major: `_render_overview_top_delay_route` hardcoded the literal word "up" immediately before the interpolated `headline['delta_pct']:g`, so an improving delay trend (a negative `delta_pct`, a routine real-world case) would render as "up -12.3%" — a factually wrong directional claim the module's own hallucination-safety contract is supposed to prevent for exactly this kind of number. Also picked up two cheap, concretely-scenario'd Minors from the same reviewer while in the file: added `test_overview_top_delay_route_shows_down_when_delay_improved` (asserts "down 12.3%", no "up", no literal "-12.3") and `test_overview_top_delay_route_requires_nonempty_routes` (an explicit `{"routes": [], "delayed_count": 0}` payload, so the dedicated empty-list `KeyError` guard at the top of the function is actually exercised instead of always short-circuiting on the missing-key case first). Left the reviewers' remaining Minors (tightening the numeric allow-list in the interpolation test to an exact set, and replacing the hand-rolled `__slots__` class with `@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)` to match `pipeline/query/router.py`'s `Rule` convention) undone as optional polish, not required for correctness. - 2026-09-05: Item 72 fix-and-reverify (pass 2) — pass 1's targeted re-verification (same two reviewer groups that found the prior Majors) confirmed both fixes fully resolved with no new edge case and no new finding. Pass 2 (fresh independent dispatch, same head `8e2c0e3`) found one new Major, independently corroborated by both reviewer groups: `_pick_template_tool`'s forced tool schema hardcoded `"params": {"type": "object"}` for every candidate, never consulting each `Template.param_schema` — the templates' own declared enum/const params contract (the entire mechanism `copilot_templates.py`'s docstring cites for "zero hallucination tolerance for numbers, by construction") never reached its only consumer, leaving `params` unconstrained for any future template with real params. Left the `locale: str = "ja"` parameter's no-op status as deferred, matching the identical seam already logged and deferred in item 71's own pass-2 entry (not a fresh regression, and wiring it through is out of this item's Task 2 scope). Re-ran `poetry run pytest tests/query/test_copilot_insight.py -v` (4/4 passed, same DATABASE_URL-default/revert technique as before), `poetry run ruff check`, and `poetry run mypy` — all clean.

Pass 2's perf/practices/comments/alternatives reviewer found one genuine Major, not caught by either pass 1 group or pass 2's own bugs/logic/consistency/security group: `check_and_consume_anon_quota`'s default-fallback logic (`daily_limit if daily_limit is not None else ask_anon_daily_limit()`, same for the IP limit) was scope-blind — it always fell back to the Ask limits regardless of `scope`, so `scope="copilot"` with no explicit override would silently enforce Ask's tighter numbers (5/day, 20/day) instead of Copilot's (20/day, 80/day) under a `"copilot:..."`-namespaced bucket key, defeating the entire point of adding `scope`. The diff's own new test (`test_ask_and_copilot_scopes_have_independent_buckets`) couldn't have caught this either, since it set `ASK_ANON_DAILY_LIMIT` and `COPILOT_ANON_DAILY_LIMIT` to the same value. Fixed by adding a module-level `_SCOPE_DEFAULT_LIMITS: dict[str, tuple[Callable[[], int], Callable[[], int]]]` mapping each scope to its own getter pair, and dispatching the default off `scope` instead of hardcoding the Ask getters; rewrote the existing test to use different limit values per scope (1 vs. 3) and assert the copilot-scoped calls actually get 3 tries with no explicit override, which fails against the old code and passes against the fix. Also fixed two Minors converged on by multiple reviewer groups across both passes: added the four tests mirroring `ask_quota_exceeded_handler`'s and `ask_anon_ip_daily_limit`'s coverage for their Copilot equivalents (`copilot_quota_exceeded_handler`'s 429/localization/fallback, `copilot_anon_ip_daily_limit`'s default/env-var-override), and reworded `api/main.py`'s comment above the `AnonCopilotQuotaExceeded` handler registration so it no longer implies the Copilot endpoint already exists (it's Task 4, not yet landed). The dispatched fix-worker reported zero write access in this worktree (Edit and any DB/git/poetry Bash command denied outright, plain read/list commands fine) — the same asymmetry items 3/70/72 hit — so the coordinator applied the fix directly via `EnterWorktree`. Verified with `make test DATABASE_URL=postgresql://transit:transit@localhost:5544/transit_test PYTEST_ADDOPTS="tests/unit/test_ratelimit.py -v"` (29/29 passed, up from 23 — the rewritten scope-independence test plus 6 new tests), `poetry run ruff check`, and `poetry run mypy` (both full-repo) — all clean. Targeted re-verification (perf/practices/comments/alternatives group) confirmed the Major is fully resolved with no new edge case. Both required `/review-branch` passes are now complete. (PR #327)

- 2026-09-05: Item 77 (Copilot Phase B Task 7 — numeric verifier module).
  Added `pipeline/query/hallucination_guard.py`'s `verify_numeric_claims(answer,
  grounding)`, which regex-extracts every number in a free-form LLM answer and
  checks each against a flattened set of numeric leaves from the grounding
  dict (exact match, or a match after rounding to the nearest int/0.1, so a
  model paraphrasing "14.2" as "about 14" isn't treated as a fabrication). An
  answer with no numbers passes trivially; any unmatched number fails the
  whole answer, since this is a structural defense-in-depth check (not a
  system-prompt instruction) ahead of Task 8's wiring into `chat_with_tools`.
  Initial worker commit added `tests/unit/test_hallucination_guard.py` and
  the module as an unmodified copy of the plan's Task 7 code block, from
  `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-ai-copilot-side-panel.md` (this file and
  its Task 9 spec companion are gitignored `docs/*` content, copied into this
  worktree from the persistent checkout per item 71's sync note). The
  coordinator's own two-pass `/review-branch` then found and fixed two Major
  gaps beyond the plan's minimal code, extending it rather than leaving it
  verbatim: (1) the plan's bare `_NUMBER_RE = r"-?\d+\.?\d*"` read a bare
  hyphen as a minus sign with no word-boundary check, so an ISO date string
  in `grounding` (e.g. `from_date`/`to_date`, which `chat.py` does add to
  context dicts) leaked spurious negative numbers into the allowed set, and a
  hyphen-glued token like "route-14" in an answer was misread as -14; fixed
  via a split-alternative `_NUMBER_RE`, a new `_DATE_RE` stripping
  `\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}` substrings before extraction, and a new
  `_THOUSANDS_SEP_RE` for comma-grouped numbers, folded into a shared
  `_extract_numbers` helper. (2) `_flatten_numbers` didn't recognize
  `decimal.Decimal`, which is exactly what asyncpg hands back for a Postgres
  `NUMERIC` column (e.g. `agg_hour_daily.avg_min`) — a real tool-result value
  this guard will see once Task 8 wires it in — so a correct answer quoting
  such a value would have been wrongly rejected as a hallucination; fixed by
  adding `decimal.Decimal` to the numeric-leaf check. 5 new tests were added
  covering these cases (dates, hyphen-glued and letter-glued tokens, comma
  thousands-separators, `Decimal` values), for 9 total. `_flatten_numbers`
  also gained a `value: object` type annotation and had its dead
  `try/except ValueError` around `float()` removed. A documented,
  non-blocking residual from review: `verify_numeric_claims`'s match loop is
  O(len(claimed) × len(allowed)), recomputing `round()` per claimed number
  instead of once total — left as-is since the module isn't wired into any
  live caller yet. Verification: `poetry run pytest
  tests/unit/test_hallucination_guard.py -v` (9/9 passed), `poetry run ruff
  check`, and `poetry run mypy`, all run from the main checkout pointed at
  this worktree's file paths (the dispatched worker's own sandbox denies
  `poetry`, a documented `transit-app-gotchas` limitation) — all clean. (PR
  #331)
- 2026-09-05: Wired `pipeline.query.hallucination_guard.verify_numeric_claims`
  into `chat_with_tools` (`pipeline/query/chat.py`). Enumerated every return
  site in the function (and its `_dispatch_and_respond` helper and the
  build-mode/intent-cache branches) and found exactly one where `answer` is
  genuinely LLM-authored free text — the out-of-scope refusal/suggestion path
  (no tool called, `tool_calls` empty, non-empty `msg.content`); every other
  site either returns a canned locale string or `render_tool_result`'s
  deterministic formatting of an already-dispatched, already-grounded
  `ToolResult`, so those are correctly left unguarded. Added a
  `_numeric_guard(answer, grounding, locale)` helper called at that one site
  with `grounding={}`: since no tool was dispatched, there is no data to
  trace a number back to, so any digit in the reply is treated as
  unverifiable and replaced with the new `numeric_guard_fallback` locale
  string (added to `pipeline/query/tools.py`'s `_LOCALES`, read via its
  existing `_summary` accessor rather than adding a second lookup
  mechanism); `grounding=None` is kept as a separate, reserved sentinel that
  skips the check entirely, for a future call site with no grounding concept
  at all. (Fixed from the worker's initial cut, which called this site with
  `grounding=None` and so never actually exercised `verify_numeric_claims`
  in production — caught in review as a structural no-op.) Added a
  `numeric_guard_triggered: bool` key to all thirteen of the function's
  return dicts. Created `tests/query/test_chat_hallucination_guard.py` (13
  tests): direct accept/reject/skip coverage of `_numeric_guard` for both
  the `None` and `{}` grounding sentinels, plus wiring checks that every
  real `chat_with_tools` return path carries the new key with the expected
  value, that a fabricated number in an out-of-scope reply is replaced, and
  that a benign out-of-scope reply with no numbers passes through
  unchanged. Verification: `poetry run pytest
  tests/query/test_chat_hallucination_guard.py -v` (13/13 passed), `poetry

  tests/api/test_ask_endpoints.py -v` (301 passed, 22 skipped — ClickHouse-

- 2026-09-06: Added the BYOK provider-key validation call (item 83, Copilot plan Phase D Task 13). New `pipeline/query/llm_key_validation.py` exposes `async def validate_provider_key(provider, api_key) -> bool`, which rejects providers outside `user_llm_keys.ALLOWED_PROVIDERS`, otherwise makes a 1-token `openai.AsyncOpenAI` chat-completion probe against that provider's `llm_client._PROVIDER_DEFAULTS` `base_url`/`model`, treating `AuthenticationError`/`PermissionDeniedError` as an invalid key, re-raising `APIConnectionError`/`APITimeoutError` since a network failure proves nothing about the key, and treating any other `APIError` as a transient provider hiccup (valid key); the client is closed in a `finally` block. `tests/unit/test_llm_key_validation.py` mocks `openai.AsyncOpenAI` entirely (no real network calls) and covers all four branches; its invalid-key case constructs a bare `AuthenticationError` subclass with `__init__` bypassed, since the installed SDK's real constructor dereferences `response.request` and the code under test only ever type-checks the exception.
