- 2026-09-09: Item 104: added a per-route "minimum performance standard" bonus/malus simulation on top of item 94's Excess Waiting Time and item 98's vehicle-km-delivered rate. Migration `0041_route_performance_standards` adds a manually-populated `route_performance_standards` table (agency_id, route_code, metric_type in `ewt_sec`/`vehicle_km_delivered_pct`, threshold_value, bonus_malus_rate), following the same operator-populated-table convention as `ridership_weights`. `pipeline/reports/performance_standard.py`'s `compute_performance_standards` joins each configured row against `pipeline.reports.headway_quality`'s per-route EWT (`ewt_sec`, route-scoped) or `pipeline.reports.supply`'s vehicle-km-delivered rate (agency-scoped only, since no per-route breakdown exists — surfaced via a `metric_scope` field), and derives a signed relative deviation (`1.0` = exactly at standard, positive = bonus, negative = deduction) that's `None` for an unresolvable actual value or a zero threshold rather than a fabricated number. A new `GET /api/{agency_id}/performance_standards` endpoint and `PerformanceStandardPanel` frontend component (mounted alongside `HeadwayQualityPanel` on the `on_time` report tab) surface this, both carrying a server-rendered localized disclaimer plus a client-side i18n badge (`reports.performance_standard.simulation_badge`) making clear this is an internal simulation/estimate, never an actual invoice or contractual output. Verified: a synthetic route exactly at its EWT threshold and one exactly at its vehicle-km-delivered threshold both yield a zero estimate; routes below either threshold yield the expected signed deduction; a zero threshold and a route with no resolvable actual value both degrade to `None` rather than crashing. Backend: `poetry run ruff check`/`format --check`, `poetry run mypy` on all touched files, and the full `make test` suite against the throwaway `:5544` Postgres DB. Frontend: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint` (0 errors), `npm run lint:i18n`, `npm run lint:i18n-strings`, the full `npm run test` suite (571/571 passed), `npm run test:check-entry-chunk`, and `npm run build:bundle && npm run check:entry-chunk` (502.5 KiB entry static closure, MapLibre-free) — all clean. Resumed and shipped by a later `/vps-loop-run` tick after a human reconciled several stale stashes left on this worktree from an interrupted prior resume; both required `/review-branch` passes are now clean (one Minor perf finding — the `ewt_sec` resolution path recomputes headway quality over every high-frequency route instead of just the configured ones — and one Minor practices finding — an unused `MetricType` type alias — both non-blocking). (PR #366)
- 2026-09-09: Item 105: added the representative weather-based metric item 100's feasibility spike recommended GO on -- a rain-vs-dry delay comparison built strictly on historical observation, never a forecast. Migration `0042_weather_observations` adds `agency_weather_stations` (one operator-chosen, documented representative station per agency, PK on agency_id, following the `ridership_weights`/`route_performance_standards` operator-populated convention; its `note` is a public, operator-facing field returned verbatim by the report endpoint) and `weather_daily_observations` (keyed by `(station_id, obs_date)` so agencies sharing a station share one fetch, with `retrieved_at` as the revision marker and `source` carrying machine-readable provenance). `pipeline/weather.py` ingests JMA AMeDAS 10-minute point observations, aggregating a day whole-or-not-at-all (a partial precipitation sum would mis-file a wet day as dry), confining every request to the source's publication window -- a stored day is re-fetched inside it to absorb the source's revisions and its availability lag, while past it nothing is asked for at all, neither a revision nor a first copy of a day that was never stored, and the same window caps how far back a pass may walk -- rejecting non-finite values at parse time (Postgres treats `'NaN'::float8 >= 0` and `'Infinity'::float8 >= 0` as true, so the stored CHECK cannot catch them), and bounding both a single fetched block and the merged nine-block total in bytes since the cron path runs it inside the long-lived API process. A pass also takes a monotonic-clock `max_seconds` budget (mandatory on the cron path, which shares its connection's session-level ingest+analyze lock with the live GTFS-RT polls a held lock would drop) and commits each station-day on its own, so no transaction is ever open across an outbound fetch and a station that fails part-way keeps the days it already committed. Every outbound fetch is gated by the `WEATHER_INGEST_ENABLED` kill switch (default off; disabled is a graceful no-op that reads as "not available", the same as an agency with no station configured), exposed as `gtfs_pipeline.py ingest_weather` / `make ingest-weather` and scheduled from `api/routers/internal.py`'s cron path. `pipeline/reports/weather.py`'s `compute_rain_delay` splits in-range service days at 1 mm observed daily rainfall and reports each side's pooled average delay (exact raw-seconds sum over sample count, never an average of per-day averages) plus the difference, excluding days with no observation from both sides rather than assuming them dry, and flagging thin sides low-confidence; `GET /api/{agency_id}/weather_delay` returns it with a localized plain-language disclaimer (observation, not forecast; association, not cause) and the source-plus-processing attribution JMA's public-data terms require. No frontend panel: item 105 asks only for the metric, so only the response types land in `frontend/src/api/types.ts`. Verified: `ruff check` and `ruff format --check` clean repo-wide, `mypy` clean (108 source files), `tests/unit/test_weather.py` + `tests/api/test_weather_delay.py` (44 passed) and the full backend suite against the throwaway `:5544` Postgres and `:8124` ClickHouse with `RUN_CH_INTEGRATION=1`; frontend `npm run typecheck`, `test` (571/571), `lint` (0 errors), `lint:i18n`, `lint:i18n-strings`, `test:check-entry-chunk`, and `build:bundle && check:entry-chunk` (502.5 KiB entry static closure, MapLibre-free) all green. (PR #369)
- 2026-09-10: Item 106: gave the Oracle collector real alerting instead of a cron.log nobody reads. New `oracle_cloud/v3/bin/health-check.sh` (cron, every 30 min) re-derives from what is on disk whether each agency in `etc/agencies.tsv` is still producing non-empty RT (newest `TripUpdate_*.pb` younger than `interval * RT_STALE_FACTOR`, at least `MIN_RT_BYTES`), whether static fetching still succeeds, and whether `sync-r2.sh`'s `.sync-r2.last-ok` marker is fresh -- gated on the same `SYNC_R2_MAX_STALE_DAYS` `prune.sh` refuses to run past, so the threshold that stops pruning and the one that pages can't drift apart -- and pings a healthchecks.io check (`ALERT_PING_URL`, the same convention the per-agency pollers already use) with the bare URL on all-clear and `/fail` plus the specific reasons otherwise; running it on a short interval also makes the check's own period/grace alarm cover the VM or cron itself dying. New `bin/cron-wrap.sh` wraps every cron job so a nonzero exit pings `/fail` with the job name, status, and output tail while cron.log still gets the full output verbatim (failure-only, since health-check.sh owns the all-clear and a per-job success ping would keep re-clearing a still-broken check). Static staleness needed a new signal: `latest.zip`'s mtime only moves when upstream *content* changes, so `static-fetch.sh` now writes a per-agency `.static-last-ok` on every successful fetch regardless of change, and collects per-agency failures into a nonzero exit (previously it swallowed them) so the wrapper can page. Both new scripts share `bin/alert-lib.sh`, whose unset-`ALERT_PING_URL` path degrades to the previous log-only behavior without changing any caller's exit status, and which never echoes the check URL (it is a bearer token). Fixed a pre-existing parsing bug found while writing the roster reader: tab is an IFS *whitespace* character, so `IFS=$'\t' read` collapses a run of tabs and shifts every column after an empty one left -- agency 1's real row (empty `static_url`, non-empty `ping_url`) made `rt-poller.sh` run with no healthcheck ping at all and `static-fetch.sh` curl that agency's hc-ping URL and store the response as its GTFS zip; new `bin/agencies-lib.sh` translates tabs to a non-whitespace separator first and is now the single reader for all three scripts. Verified: all nine `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/*.sh` suites pass, including new `test_health_check.sh` (17 cases: healthy all-clear, stale/empty/absent RT, an agency configured but never started, static staleness following the marker rather than an unchanged zip's mtime, tiny or dangling `latest.zip`, missing/stale R2 marker, empty and missing roster, unconfigured and undeliverable alerting, bad threshold) and `test_cron_wrap.sh`, plus new regression cases in `test_rt_poller.sh`/`test_static_fetch.sh` for the empty-column shift; `bash -n` clean on every touched script (no shellcheck available in this environment). No Python/TypeScript touched. (PR #394)
- 2026-09-10: Item 107: replaced the hardcoded `RT_FIELD_COVERAGE_CONFIRMED_AGENCIES` frozenset with a durable probe registry, so RT-optional-field report availability follows verified feed state instead of a code change plus deploy. Migration `0043_rt_field_coverage_probes` adds a per-(agency, field) table carrying the verdict, the measured coverage fraction and sample size, the source feed probed, `probed_at`, and a nullable `expires_at` (NULL = does not expire), seeded from the previously-hardcoded 8/9/10 as non-expiring rows keyed off each agency's own `feed_url` — a no-op on a database where those ids are absent or not `static_join`. `pipeline/strategies/static_join.py` keeps the single canonical gate: `rt_field_coverage_confirmed` (and a new batch `rt_field_coverage_confirmed_agencies`, which `pipeline/reports/service_delivered.py` now uses in place of its own ingest-strategy query plus set intersection) requires an `RT_INGEST_STRATEGIES` ingest strategy AND a live, affirmative verdict for every one of the four `RT_COVERAGE_FIELDS`; an expired, refuted, missing, or partial verdict all read as "not available" rather than as trust. The threshold logic that turns measured fractions into a verdict moved out of the probe CLI into `assess_field_coverage`, next to the decoder and the gate, so the CLI's human-facing report and the recorded row can't disagree; `record_field_coverage_probe` upserts all four fields in one transaction and refuses a zero-`stop_time_updates` capture outright, since an overnight poll is not evidence and would otherwise become a durable refutation. `scripts/probe_rt_field_coverage.py` gains `--agency-id/--record/--ttl-days` (default 180 days, `0` = non-expiring) to persist a verdict, still needing no DB at all without `--record`. Tests moved from monkeypatching the removed constant to a shared `tests/conftest.py::confirm_rt_field_coverage` helper that records real registry rows and deliberately leaves `ingest_strategy` to the caller, and a new `tests/pipeline/test_rt_coverage_registry.py` covers the unprobed default, expiry, refutation, re-probe withdrawal, partial verdicts, the ingest-strategy half, the empty-capture refusal, and batch/single agreement. Verified: `ruff format`/`ruff check` and `scripts/comment_lint.py --diff main` clean, `mypy` clean on the touched modules, `tests/pipeline/test_rt_coverage_registry.py` + `tests/unit/test_probe_rt_field_coverage.py` + `tests/db` (44 passed), the affected API/query/pipeline modules green against the throwaway `:5544` Postgres and `:8124` ClickHouse with `RUN_CH_INTEGRATION=1`, and a scratch-database `migrate up`/`down`/`up` round trip confirming the seed lands for 8/9/10 only. Review follow-ups closed four gaps in the same shape as the change itself: `--record` now reads the agency's stored `feed_url` and refuses (writing nothing) a `--url` probe that isn't it, since these vendor feeds differ only by an operator number in the path and a mistyped `--agency-id` would otherwise hand one operator four affirmative verdicts earned by another's feed, while `--file` stays exempt by construction and says so, being the deliberate local-capture escape hatch; `PATCH /api/agencies/{id}` deletes an agency's probe rows inside its existing transaction whenever the write genuinely repoints `feed_url` (resubmitting the current URL does not), through a new canonical `invalidate_field_coverage_probes` in `static_join.py`, so a never-probed feed can no longer inherit the previous feed's verdict -- and `patch_agency` is the only writer that can repoint an existing row, as `cmd_seed_agencies` upserts on `feed_url` itself and never updates it; the carry-over seed matches the three feeds' own `feed_url` values rather than agency_ids 8/9/10, which on a deployment whose agencies came from the admin API's `SERIAL` are merely the 8th-10th rows inserted, so the guard now does what its comment claims; and the `arr_delay` band's per-field detail moved into `assess_field_coverage`'s docstring, next to where the fields are decided, clearing `comment_lint`'s block limit. Each is pinned by a test: stub-connection cases in `tests/unit/test_probe_rt_field_coverage.py` for the source/agency mismatch, unknown agency, matching URL and capture paths; repoint/no-op/other-field cases in `tests/api/test_api_agencies.py` plus a gate-closing case in `tests/pipeline/test_rt_coverage_registry.py`; and a new `tests/db/test_rt_coverage_seed.py` that runs the migration's own INSERT against agencies carrying those feeds at other ids, another operator holding ids 8/9/10, a carried-over feed moved to a different ingest strategy, and an unrelated database. (PR #381)
- 2026-09-10: Item 108: made `NEXT_TASK.md` self-healing instead of relying on every tick to remember to update it. New `scripts/reconcile_next_task.py` does two independent, narrow repairs: (1) for any backlog item whose `vps-loop/item-<N>` branch already has a merged PR but whose own bold header doesn't yet open with `DONE`/`MOOT`/`DO NOT START`, prefixes it with a normalized `DONE (PR #<number>, merged <date>) — ` marker, never touching anything beyond that prefix; (2) merges any `## ` heading whose exact title repeats (e.g. a second "Restored Backlog Entries" section from an unaware future restoration) into the first occurrence, correctly treating any heading level as a section boundary so an unrelated `# Status log` sitting between two same-titled `## ` sections is never absorbed into the body being moved. Wired into `/vps-loop-run` as a new Step 2a, run every tick before item selection. Also split Step 6.11 into 6.11 (edit the merged item's own header to `DONE` from the main checkout, right after `/cleanup-merged`) and 6.12 (the pre-existing Status log append), since the coordinator's log entries already did the header edit by convention but the written process never required it — formalizing that step is what keeps the backlog authoritative even on a tick that dies between merge and log-write, with Step 2a as the later-tick backstop if 6.11 is ever skipped. Verified: new `tests/unit/test_reconcile_next_task.py` (parsing, terminal-marker detection, merge-payload parsing, status reconciliation including the already-marked/no-matching-item/differently-worded-heading edge cases, duplicate-section merging across an intervening unrelated section, and CLI dry-run/apply/missing-file paths), plus the existing `tests/unit/test_cleanup_git_state.py` and `tests/unit/test_daily_git_hygiene.py` (whose helpers this script reuses via the same `importlib`-by-path pattern `daily_git_hygiene.py` already established), all passing; `ruff check`/`mypy` clean on the new module and its test file; the full `tests/unit/` suite unaffected. No live `gh`/database calls were exercised against the real, untracked `NEXT_TASK.md` — that file is intentionally never touched by this branch's own commits. Two `/review-branch` findings were fixed during review (a duplicate-item-number handling gap that silently guessed which copy to mark `DONE`, and a related fence-awareness gap in the item scanner); two cosmetic Minors remain as documented residual risk: the fence scanner doesn't track which delimiter opened a fence, so a mismatched-delimiter edge case could reopen unfenced parsing mid-block (dormant today, since this file only uses matched triple-backtick fences), and the reconciler can print two differently-worded warnings for the same duplicated-item-number fact. (PR #382)
- 2026-09-11: Item 109: closed the gap where the Oracle collector's only evidence that R2 actually held fresh, intact data was `sync-r2.sh`'s own `aws s3 sync` exit status. New `oracle_cloud/v3/bin/verify-r2.sh` (cron, daily, right after `sync-r2.sh`) lists each configured agency's actual `rt/<id>/` and `static/<id>/` R2 objects directly and checks them independently: the newest RT object exists, is younger than `R2_RT_MAX_STALE_DAYS`, is at least `MIN_RT_OBJECT_BYTES`, and — when the same file is still on local disk — matches its local byte size exactly; the R2 object matching each agency's current local `latest.zip` target is actually present, at least `MIN_STATIC_OBJECT_BYTES`, and also byte-size-matches the local file. Byte-size equality stands in for a checksum here since `s3api head-object` ETags aren't reliable against multipart uploads and a truncated/corrupted transfer almost always changes the size anyway. New `oracle_cloud/v3/bin/prune-r2.sh` (cron, weekly, gated on the same fresh-`sync-r2.sh`-marker check `prune.sh` uses) replaces the previous "R2 objects are never pruned" unbounded-growth tradeoff with a bounded, deliberately multi-year retention window (`R2_RT_RETENTION_DAYS` default 1825, `R2_STATIC_RETENTION_DAYS` default 3650) since R2 is the durable long-term archive, not local disk's 90-day rolling buffer — it never deletes the object matching an agency's current `latest.zip` target regardless of age, mirroring `prune.sh`'s own live-target rule. Both scripts reuse `agencies-lib.sh`'s tab-safe roster reader rather than a naive `IFS=$'\t' read` (the exact parsing bug item 106 fixed elsewhere) and isolate one agency's or one object's failure from the rest of the run, matching `sync-r2.sh`'s existing pattern. `crontab.snippet` and `MIGRATION.md` updated for both new jobs; `sync-r2.sh`'s header comment updated to point at them instead of describing the now-stale unbounded-growth acceptance. Verified: new `tests/test_verify_r2.sh` (11 cases: a fresh intact mirror, missing env fails closed, missing/stale RT objects, RT and static byte-size mismatches, an un-mirrored current static target, a too-small static object, empty/missing roster, bad threshold) and `tests/test_prune_r2.sh` (6 cases: refuses without/with a stale marker, prunes old objects while keeping young ones and the live static target, the generous default retention deletes nothing, one delete failure doesn't abort the run but still exits nonzero, missing roster) against a fake `aws` CLI shim extended to answer `s3 ls`/`s3 rm`; all eight pre-existing `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/*.sh` suites still pass unmodified; `bash -n` clean on every touched script (no shellcheck available in this environment). No Python/TypeScript touched. (PR #383)
- 2026-09-11: Item 109 follow-up: `prune-r2.sh`'s validation accepted `0` as a "positive integer" for `R2_RT_RETENTION_DAYS`/`R2_STATIC_RETENTION_DAYS`, and with retention `0` the RT path (which has no live-target keep exception) would delete every RT object ever uploaded on its very first run. The digits-only `case` pattern now also rejects a bare `0`, matching the check's own error message; `tests/test_prune_r2.sh` gained two regression cases (`R2_RT_RETENTION_DAYS=0` and `R2_STATIC_RETENTION_DAYS=0` both exit 64 with no `aws` call made). Full `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/*.sh` suite (10 files) still passes; `bash -n` clean on the touched script.
- 2026-09-11: Item 109 second follow-up: a leading-zero numeral like `R2_RT_RETENTION_DAYS=00` passed the digits-only `case` check (it's neither empty, non-digit, nor the literal string `0`) but still reproduced the exact same catastrophic bug, since `cutoff=$(( NOW - retention * 86400 ))` evaluates it in a plain arithmetic context where a leading zero means octal, and `00` (like `000`, etc.) is octal `0` too. Replaced the lexical `|0)` case arm with a numeric check forcing base-10 interpretation (`$((10#$value))`) and rejecting anything `-le 0`, run after the existing empty/non-digit `case` so both failure modes still short-circuit before any arithmetic. Added a `R2_RT_RETENTION_DAYS=00` regression case to `tests/test_prune_r2.sh` alongside the existing `=0` case; full `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/*.sh` suite (10 files) still passes; `bash -n` clean on the touched script.
- 2026-09-11: Item 109 third follow-up: `00`/`0` weren't the only leading-zero values a plain arithmetic context misreads as octal -- a non-degenerate one like `010` or `018` still slipped through untouched. `verify-r2.sh` had no equivalent hardening at all (`R2_RT_MAX_STALE_DAYS=008` reached `rt_max=$(( R2_RT_MAX_STALE_DAYS * 86400 ))` at face value, hit octal's invalid digit 8, and aborted the whole per-agency `while` loop via a fatal "value too great for base" while the script still exited 0 and reported "R2 fresh and intact"; `=010` would have silently applied an 8-day threshold instead of 10). `prune-r2.sh`'s own validation forced base-10 only to check `-gt 0`, never writing the normalized value back, so `R2_RT_RETENTION_DAYS=018` still crashed `cutoff=$(( NOW - retention * 86400 ))` in `prune_prefix` and `=010` still silently pruned two days early. Both scripts now reassign each validated variable to its canonical decimal form (`printf -v "$var" '%d' "$((10#$value))"`) immediately after validation, so every downstream plain-arithmetic use — including `prune_prefix`'s `retention="$2"` parameter — only ever sees a clean decimal string. Also closed a related gap in `prune-r2.sh`: when an agency's local `latest.zip` symlink can't be resolved, `keep` came back empty and `prune_prefix`'s keep-guard then protected nothing, so every one of that agency's static objects past retention -- including R2's only copy -- would be deleted; static pruning now `continue`s (skips) for that agency instead, matching local `prune.sh`'s "unknown live target means don't touch it" behavior. `tests/test_verify_r2.sh` gained two regression cases (`R2_RT_MAX_STALE_DAYS=010` correctly keeps a 9-day-old RT object fresh instead of misreading the limit as octal 8; `=008` no longer aborts the per-agency loop) and `tests/test_prune_r2.sh` gained three (`R2_RT_RETENTION_DAYS=010` keeps a 9-day-old object instead of pruning it under a misread 8-day retention; `=018` no longer crashes; an agency whose `latest.zip` can't be resolved has its static objects left untouched). Full `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/*.sh` suite (10 files, 76 cases total) passes; `bash -n` clean on every touched script.
- 2026-09-11: Item 110: isolated CI integration resources per job/worktree. New `scripts/run_full_ci.sh` builds and starts a uniquely-named Postgres + ClickHouse pair on two OS-assigned free ports for a single invocation, applies schema, runs the same lint/type/test gate as `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `test` job, and always tears both containers down on exit — closing the collision the shared, fixed-name/fixed-port `transit-test-pg`/`transit-test-ch` pair has when two runs (e.g. two worktrees on the same VPS) overlap, whose per-test Postgres `TRUNCATE ... CASCADE` and ClickHouse `DROP TABLE`/`CREATE TABLE` otherwise race across the two runs (a real occurrence — see this file's item 104 entry). `scripts/run_integration_tests.sh` gained `TEST_PG_PORT`/`TEST_CH_PORT` overrides (defaulting to the existing shared `:5544`/`:8124` pair, so every existing caller is unaffected) so it can be pointed at that isolated pair; it also now exports `CLICKHOUSE_TEST_PORT` alongside `CLICKHOUSE_PORT`, closing a latent gap where the two could silently diverge. Verified directly against Docker: two concurrent `scripts/run_full_ci.sh` invocations get distinct container names and ports with no collision, both apply schema and pass `ruff check`/`mypy` independently, and both tear down cleanly, all without touching the pre-existing shared `transit-test-pg`/`transit-test-ch` containers; `scripts/run_integration_tests.sh`'s new port overrides were confirmed by running `tests/pipeline/test_analyze.py` (44 passed) against a manually-started isolated pair. Both full end-to-end runs of `run_full_ci.sh` itself correctly stopped at its `ruff format --check` step on pre-existing, unrelated formatting drift in `scripts/reconcile_next_task.py`/`tests/unit/test_reconcile_next_task.py` already present on `main` (from item 108, untouched by this change) — left as-is since fixing it is outside this item's scope. No Python touched, so `mypy`/the full `pytest` suite were not re-run repo-wide beyond the subset above. (PR #384)
- 2026-09-11: Item 112: made the weather ingest's wall-clock budget on the cron path actually bound what it claims to bound. `ingest_weather`'s `max_seconds` was only ever consulted between blocks, and `_FETCH_TIMEOUT_SEC` re-arms on every socket read, so a source trickling its response body a few bytes at a time could hold one already-started read open past the deadline indefinitely; on the cron path that call ran on the same connection that held the ingest+analyze session-level advisory lock, so the overrun kept the lock taken and every cron poke arriving meanwhile took the "already in flight" path, dropping live GTFS-RT polls that can't be recovered once their moment has passed. `api/routers/internal.py`'s `_run_ingest_and_analyze` no longer calls `ingest_weather` from inside the block holding that lock's connection; the call moved into a new `_run_weather_ingest(db_url)`, invoked unconditionally right after the `finally` block -- unconditionally, because every path that should skip it (a lock miss, an empty roster, or a setup failure) already `return`s or raises from inside the `try` above, which runs `finally` and exits the whole function immediately; execution reaches the call at all only when the lock was held and agencies were found. The freshness check (`check_agg_freshness`) is now wrapped in its own `try`/`except`, matching the per-agency ingest/analyze loop's existing isolation pattern: previously an unhandled exception there would propagate out of the whole function, which would have skipped the relocated weather pass too now that it sits after this check rather than before it. `_run_weather_ingest` opens its own fresh `psycopg2` connection, calls `ingest_weather(weather_conn, max_seconds=CRON_INGEST_BUDGET_SEC)`, and closes that connection in its own `finally` — independent of the ingest+analyze session throughout, which `ingest_weather` was already able to tolerate since it commits per station-day and reads nothing from that transaction. Releasing the lock before this call also means two closely-spaced cron pokes can now each run their own weather pass concurrently rather than being serialized by the advisory lock as before; `ingest_weather`'s per-station-day upsert already tolerates this (documented in `_run_weather_ingest`'s own docstring), so the trade-off is a possible duplicate outbound fetch, never incorrect data. `pipeline/weather.py`'s comments on `_FETCH_TIMEOUT_SEC`, `CRON_INGEST_BUDGET_SEC`, and `ingest_weather`'s docstring were updated to describe the budget as this pass's own runtime ceiling rather than a lock-protection measure, since a slow source can no longer hold anyone else's lock by construction; a rollback-before-reraise comment that cited the now-removed shared-connection freshness check as its rationale was reworded to state the general reason instead. `tests/api/test_internal_cron.py` gained coverage for both directions: a lock-miss poke and a successful poke that reaches the per-agency loop; and a new regression test drives `ingest_weather` with a fake that opens a second, independent connection and probes `pg_try_advisory_lock` on the ingest+analyze lock key from inside the weather call itself, confirming the lock is actually free (not just "expected to be") at the moment the weather pass runs. Verified against the throwaway `:5544` Postgres and `:8124` ClickHouse (`RUN_CH_INTEGRATION=1`): `tests/api/test_internal_cron.py` (7 passed), `tests/unit/test_weather.py` (38 passed), and `tests/pipeline/test_freshness.py` (14 passed, since two of its tests drive `_run_ingest_and_analyze` end-to-end and now also exercise the new post-lock weather call path, which stays a no-op without `WEATHER_INGEST_ENABLED` set). `ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, and `mypy` all clean on every touched file (`api/routers/internal.py`, `pipeline/weather.py`, `tests/api/test_internal_cron.py`). (PR #388)
- 2026-09-11: Item 113: bounded the weather ingest's parsed block SHAPE, not only its wire bytes. `_fetch_block` now rejects (returns `None`, same as any other unreadable block) a parsed mapping with more than `_MAX_READINGS_PER_BLOCK` (90, several times the ~18 readings a real 3-hour block holds) entries, since a small-key payload well under the byte cap could still merge into a much larger `readings` dict of live Python objects in the long-lived API process across a station-day's nine blocks. Also lowered `_MAX_BLOCK_BYTES` (8 MiB to 256 KiB) and `_MAX_DAY_BYTES` (16 MiB to 2 MiB), which previously sat roughly two orders of magnitude above any legitimate payload. Verified: new `tests/unit/test_weather.py` cases cover a real-sized (18-entry) block passing, a block exactly at the shape cap passing, and one over the cap being rejected; `ruff check`, `mypy`, and the full `tests/unit/` suite (753 passed) plus `tests/api/test_weather_delay.py` and `tests/api/test_internal_cron.py` (21 passed) against the throwaway `:5544` Postgres and `:8124` ClickHouse with `RUN_CH_INTEGRATION=1` all clean. (PR #389)
- 2026-09-11: Item 114: `_fetch_block` now validates `station_id` against an explicit `[0-9]{5}` character set (matching every real AMeDAS station number) before building the URL, rather than relying on `quote(station_id, safe="")` alone -- that encoding leaves `.` untouched, so a `..` dot-segment value survived into the URL path and let most origin servers resolve the fetch one directory up, contradicting the docstring's and `test_fetch_block_percent_encodes_the_station_id`'s claim that encoding fully bounded the fetched document. The check uses `fullmatch` against an unanchored pattern rather than `match` against a `$`-terminated one, since a bare `$` anchor matches before a trailing newline too. `tests/unit/test_weather.py` replaced that test with one confirming a valid id is still percent-encoded, plus new rejection cases for a path-separator id, a dot-segment id, and a trailing-newline id. Verified: `ruff check`, `mypy`, and the full `tests/unit/` suite (756 passed) clean. (PR #393)
- 2026-09-11: Item 115: added `oracle_cloud/v3/bin/spool-cleanup.sh`, an idempotent daily job that reclaims local RT/static archives file by file the moment its own R2 listing confirms (byte-size match, the same technique `verify-r2.sh` uses) that specific archive is uploaded, rather than waiting out `prune.sh`'s much longer age-based retention window. A failed or partially uploaded archive (missing from R2, or present with a mismatched size) and the static `latest.zip` target are always left in place; local bytes still on disk after reclaiming everything confirmed are checked against the new required `SPOOL_DISK_BUDGET_BYTES` env var, with an overage reported as a failure and never as a further deletion. Discovers which `data/<id>/{rt,static}` directories to inspect by globbing what actually exists on disk (matching `sync-r2.sh`/`prune.sh`), not by reading `agencies.tsv`, so a directory whose agency ID has fallen out of (or was never in) the roster still gets its bytes measured and reclaimed. Wired into `crontab.snippet` right after `verify-r2.sh`, cross-referenced from `prune.sh`'s header, and documented in `MIGRATION.md` (which no longer claims `spool-cleanup.sh`'s crontab.snippet line differs from `prune.sh`'s own line in being active by default — both always were). New `tests/test_spool_cleanup.sh` covers required-env failures, reclaiming only R2-verified archives while keeping pending/corrupt uploads and the live static target, an orphaned agency directory absent from any roster still being discovered and reclaimed, idempotent re-runs, and the disk-budget-exceeded failure path without extra deletion. Full `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/*.sh` suite (11 files) passes; `bash -n` clean on every touched script. (PR #394)
- 2026-09-11: Item 116: added `oracle_cloud/v3/bin/reconcile-r2.sh`, closing the one gap `prune-r2.sh`'s age-based retention leaves open: it only ever looks at `rt/<id>/`/`static/<id>/` prefixes for ids still present in the *current* `agencies.tsv`, so an object left behind by a retired or renumbered agency, or one whose filename doesn't match the `rt/<id>/<YYYYMMDD>.tar.gz` / `static/<id>/gtfs_static_<YYYYMMDD>.zip` naming rule at all, is never pruned by anything. The new script lists the entire bucket once and classifies every object "expected" or "orphan" against the roster and that naming rule. Dry-run by default (reports orphans, exits nonzero so `cron-wrap.sh` pages on them, deletes nothing); `--execute` (or `RECONCILE_R2_EXECUTE=1`) is required to delete, itself gated on a fresh `sync-r2.sh` success marker, and re-lists and reclassifies each object individually immediately before its own delete to close the window between the initial bucket-wide listing and that delete (the roster or the object itself may have changed in between). Guards against a subtler bug caught while testing: a prefix-listing recheck on an object's own key can also return a longer key that merely starts with it (e.g. `foo.tar.gz.tmp` for prefix `foo.tar.gz`), so the recheck picks the line whose key matches exactly rather than trusting the first line returned. Also gained the same `flock`-based overlap guard `sync-r2.sh`/`spool-cleanup.sh` use. Wired into `crontab.snippet` weekly, right after `prune-r2.sh`, deliberately without `--execute` (deletion stays a manual, reviewed step) and documented in `MIGRATION.md`. New `tests/test_reconcile_r2.sh` (13 cases) covers required-env/roster failures, every orphan category, the marker-freshness gate, the exact-key recheck (including the prefix-collision case), a delete-time race, and the overlap guard. Full `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/*.sh` suite (12 files) passes; `bash -n` clean on every touched script. (PR #395)
- 2026-09-11: Item 118: defined the operations-status contract in `scripts/ops_status.py` -- a versioned (`schema_version`), read-only status-document shape shared by every future ops producer/consumer (VPS loop, GitHub, Oracle crawler, R2: items 119-123). Each `ComponentStatus` carries `component`, `state` (`healthy`/`degraded`/`stale`/`failed`/`unknown`, derived by `classify_state` from caller-supplied per-component age thresholds and an explicit failure flag), `observed_at`, `last_success_at`, `age_seconds`, and a bounded `details` object; `classify_state` resolves to `unknown` rather than a misleading verdict when a document's own `observed_at` is too far ahead of the validating clock or its two timestamps are mutually inconsistent (clock-skew handling). `validate_details` forbids credential-like keys, raw log/traceback keys, and nested/unbounded values, and `validate_component_status`/`validate_document` enforce a 4096-byte maximum payload size independent of `details`' own structural bounds; `JSON_SCHEMA` mirrors the same contract in portable JSON Schema form for a non-Python producer, and a `--validate FILE` CLI checks a raw JSON document against it. `JSON_SCHEMA`'s `details.propertyNames` pattern inlines `_NAME_RE`'s lowercase/length identifier shape alongside the forbidden-word lookaheads (not just the lookaheads alone), so a schema-only validator rejects a mixed-/upper-case forbidden-like key (`API_KEY`, `Secret`) the same way `validate_details` does; and `_validate_freshness_invariants` enforces the `state`/`age_seconds` relationship as a true iff when `last_success_at` is present -- `age_seconds is None` if and only if `state == "unknown"` -- rather than only the one direction `classify_state` happens to exercise. `tests/unit/test_ops_status.py` covers every state transition and boundary, both clock-skew paths, every forbidden `details` shape (including schema-only case-shape rejection), the state/age_seconds iff in both directions, the payload-size bound, JSON round-tripping, and the CLI. Verified: `ruff check`/`format --check`/`mypy` clean on both touched files, and the full `tests/unit/` suite green with no DB required. (PR #397)
- 2026-09-11: Item 119: published the Oracle collector's own operations-status heartbeat. New `oracle_cloud/v3/bin/status-snapshot.sh` (cron, every 30 min) builds one `oracle_crawler` `scripts/ops_status.py`-contract document from the same on-disk evidence `health-check.sh`/`verify-r2.sh` already produce -- RT/static freshness (worst-case ratio across configured agencies vs. the existing `RT_STALE_FACTOR`/`STATIC_MAX_STALE_DAYS` thresholds), local disk usage, the `.sync-r2.last-ok` marker's age and an explicit `sync_result`, `verify-r2.sh`'s own new `.verify-r2.last-result` marker (`<iso> <ok|fail> <object_count>`, written on every completed run, not just successes) for `verify_result`/`verify_age_seconds`/`r2_object_total`, and combines the three independently-classified subsystems (RT/static/R2) into one document-level `state` by taking the worst (severity `failed > stale > degraded > unknown > healthy`; a subsystem this collector isn't configured to run at all, e.g. no agency has a `static_url`, is `not_applicable` and excluded from the comparison rather than counted as a problem) and writes it atomically (`mktemp` + `mv`). It is a pure report, never a check: unlike `health-check.sh` it never pages and always exits 0 on a well-formed document, whatever state that document reports -- only a genuinely malformed run (bad threshold, can't write the output) is a script failure. New `oracle_cloud/v3/bin/publish-status.sh` (cron, right after it) sends that document to GitHub as a `repository_dispatch` event over HTTPS, authenticated by `ORACLE_STATUS_GH_TOKEN` -- a fine-grained personal access token scoped to nothing but triggering that event, held only on Oracle and unrelated to any SSH key, so this channel never needs Oracle's SSH private key to exist anywhere else; leaving the token unset is a supported no-op (mirrors `alert-lib.sh`'s own `ALERT_PING_URL` convention) so the rest of the collector's cron behavior is completely unaffected when the channel is disabled or unreachable -- both new lines are fully independent crontab entries that nothing else depends on. New `.github/workflows/oracle-heartbeat-listener.yml` (mirroring `vps-heartbeat-listener.yml`'s own opposite-direction pattern) echoes the received document as a single compact-JSON `ORACLE_STATUS <json>` log line (re-compacted via `jq -c`, since GitHub Actions' `toJson()` always pretty-prints). New `scripts/collect_oracle_status.py` is the VPS-side other half: it reads that line back out via the VPS's own already-configured `gh` authentication (no new VPS-side credential needed), validates it against the operations-status contract, and enforces replay resistance with a small persisted `observed_at` watermark -- a document not strictly newer than the last one accepted is rejected (`OracleStatusReplayed`, carrying the parsed-but-rejected status) rather than silently re-accepted as fresh, and any channel failure (no `gh`, no run yet, a malformed line, a contract-invalid document) raises `OracleStatusUnavailable` rather than returning a fabricated status, so a caller must treat it as `unknown`, matching item 121's "API failure produces unknown, never a false green status" rule. Verified: new `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/test_status_snapshot.sh` (15 cases: healthy end-to-end, missing roster, stale RT, never-succeeded static, an all-off-VM-static roster correctly reporting `not_applicable` rather than dragging the state down, missing/stale R2 sync marker, verify-r2.sh's recorded failure vs. having never run, the R2 object total flowing through, disk usage populated, a bad threshold, atomic-write/idempotency) and `test_publish_status.sh` (7 cases: disabled/no-op, a successful publish, the token never leaking, custom repo/event type, a missing or empty document failing without ever calling curl, an unreachable channel reported without leaking the token) plus two new regression cases in `test_verify_r2.sh` for the new result marker; full `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/*.sh` suite (14 files, 125 cases) passes, `bash -n` clean on every touched script. New `tests/unit/test_collect_oracle_status.py` (18 cases) covers parsing the latest of several log lines, malformed/non-object payloads, accepting a fresh document, accepting a strictly-newer one, rejecting a replayed/older one without advancing the watermark, a corrupt watermark cache, a contract-invalid document, and every `gh`-invocation failure mode of `default_log_fetcher`. `ruff check`/`mypy` clean on both touched Python files; the full `tests/unit/` suite (928 passed) green against the throwaway `:5544`/`:8124` stack. Review fix: `_LOG_LINE_RE` was anchored to the start of a line (`^ORACLE_STATUS (.+)$`), but real `gh run view --log` output prefixes every line with `<job>\t<step>\t<timestamp> `, so the marker never actually appears at a true line start in production and `parse_latest_status` always raised `OracleStatusUnavailable` even after a successful publish; the pattern is now unanchored (`ORACLE_STATUS (.+)$` with `re.MULTILINE`) to find the marker as a substring, matching `vps-heartbeat-watchdog.yml`'s own substring-search approach for the same class of problem, and `test_collect_oracle_status.py`'s `log_with()` fixture now prefixes its lines the same way by default (with an explicit unprefixed-line case kept alongside it). Also trimmed the module docstring/comment to state the invariant directly instead of citing a specific backlog item number, and folded `main()`'s redundant local `to_json_dict` import into the existing top-level `scripts.ops_status` import. Follow-up fix: `status-snapshot.sh` was copying `.verify-r2.last-result`'s own timestamp into the document's `last_success_at`/`age_seconds` whenever verify-r2.sh's last completed run had failed -- since that marker is written on every completed run, success or failure, a failed run's document could claim `last_success_at` seconds in the past while simultaneously reporting `state:"failed"`. `verify-r2.sh` now also writes `.verify-r2.last-success` (a bare ISO8601 UTC timestamp), only on a passing run, alongside the unchanged `.verify-r2.last-result`; `status-snapshot.sh` now derives `verify_epoch` (and everything downstream that folds it into the document's overall `last_success_at`/`age_seconds`) from that success-only marker, leaving it empty -- and the document's `last_success_at`/`age_seconds` null -- when there has never been a real success, the same "no epoch without real evidence" rule `rt_state`/`static_state` already follow; `state:"failed"` paired with a null `last_success_at` is a valid combination per `_validate_freshness_invariants` in `scripts/ops_status.py`. Updated `test_verify_r2.sh` (the success marker is written and matches on success, left untouched by a later failure, and never created when there has never been a prior success) and `test_status_snapshot.sh` (a verify-r2 failure with no prior success now asserts `last_success_at:null`/`age_seconds:null`; a new case covers a failure with a genuine prior success on record still reporting that success's own timestamp, not the failed run's), plus `MIGRATION.md`'s item-12 section. Full `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/*.sh` suite (14 files) and `tests/unit/test_ops_status.py`/`tests/unit/test_collect_oracle_status.py` (120 passed) verified green. (PR #400) Minor-review fixes: `publish-status.sh`'s header comment now names `scripts/collect_oracle_status.py` directly instead of calling it "a future VPS-side reader"; `MIGRATION.md`'s status-document path corrected to `$COLLECTOR_BASE/.status/oracle-crawler-status.json` (no stray `data/` segment, matching `status-snapshot.sh`'s actual default); `test_status_snapshot.sh`'s atomic-write leftover check dropped its dead `-name '*.XXXXXX'` alternative (a literal-`X` pattern `mktemp` never produces) and now relies solely on the `??????`-wildcard clause that always did the real matching; `collect_oracle_status()` now rejects a contract-valid document for a different `ops_status.py` component (e.g. `vps_loop`) instead of silently accepting it as the Oracle heartbeat, with a new test case covering it; and `status-snapshot.sh` now reports `static_state:"unknown"` for a header-only, zero-agency `agencies.tsv`, matching the value it already reports when the roster file is missing entirely (and matching `rt_state`'s own already-unified value in both cases), instead of the previous `not_applicable`/`unknown` split for the same underlying fact, with `test_status_snapshot.sh` extended to cover both paths reporting the same thing. Full `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/*.sh` suite, `tests/unit/test_collect_oracle_status.py`/`test_ops_status.py` (121 passed), `ruff check`, and `mypy` reverified green. Main-resync fixes (after `main` advanced and was merged forward into the branch): `status-snapshot.sh` could emit a negative `age_seconds` (document-level and per-subsystem) when an independent process's own marker/mtime lands after this script's own `NOW` (e.g. a backward clock step), which `scripts/ops_status.py`'s contract rejects outright -- both are now clamped at zero, mirroring `compute_age_seconds`'s own clamping; a leading-zero `r2_object_total` from a hand-edited or differently-formatted `verify-r2.sh` marker survived verbatim into JSON, where a leading zero isn't a valid number token -- normalized via base-10 (`10#`) arithmetic; and (Major) an agency's `agencies.tsv` interval flowed straight into a bash arithmetic context, where a leading-zero value is read as octal -- either a fatal "value too great for base" crash of the whole script, or a silently wrong classification for a value that happens to be valid octal -- forced to base-10, matching the same guard already applied to every other on-disk-marker-derived integer in this file. `test_status_snapshot.sh` gained matching regression cases (leading-zero `r2_object_total`, a future-dated marker clamped to zero, and a leading-zero `agencies.tsv` interval). Minor-review fixes from the post-merge two-pass re-review: `test_publish_status.sh` now guards cleanup with `trap teardown_base EXIT` immediately after `setup_base`, matching every sibling test script's convention, instead of relying on an explicit `teardown_base` call a failed assertion could skip past; and a `test_status_snapshot.sh` comment was reworded to state the leading-zero-octal invariant directly instead of citing "previously crashed" history. Full `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/*.sh` suite (24/24, 7/7, 18/18 across the three touched files) and `tests/unit/test_collect_oracle_status.py` (19/19) reverified green; `ruff check`/`mypy` clean; `bash -n` clean on every touched script. Both required `/review-branch` passes are now clean on the merged, fully re-reviewed result. Post-merge re-sync (a later, unrelated `main` commit landed and was merged forward with no file overlap) triggered a mandatory fresh two-pass re-review; that pass found two more Minors, both fixed directly: `disk_free_bytes`/`disk_used_pct` derived from `df -Pk` fed straight into arithmetic/JSON without the same base-10 (`10#`) normalization already applied to every other on-disk-derived integer in this file, so a zero-padded field (rare in practice, but asymmetric with the stated invariant) could misclassify via octal or crash the script the same way the `agencies.tsv` interval bug did -- normalized, with a new `test_status_snapshot.sh` case shimming `df` to emit a zero-padded available-KB/capacity pair and asserting both the base-10 result and JSON validity; and the shell-built document's own test suite never validated its output against the actual Python contract it claims to satisfy (`scripts/ops_status.py`'s `validate_document`), only against generic JSON-parses-cleanly checks, so a future contract change this producer stopped satisfying would only ever have surfaced downstream as `collect_oracle_status.py` rejecting every heartbeat in production -- added two cases invoking `python3 scripts/ops_status.py --validate` directly against both a healthy and a failed-with-no-prior-success document. Full `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/*.sh` suite (14 files) and `tests/unit/test_collect_oracle_status.py`/`test_ops_status.py` (121 passed) reverified green; `ruff check` clean; `bash -n` clean on every touched script. Pass 2 of the post-resync re-review found one Major (enforcement/security) and one Minor (practices), both fixed directly: the "narrowly-scoped ... nothing but triggering `repository_dispatch`" claim repeated in the workflow header, `publish-status.sh`'s header comment, and `MIGRATION.md` was factually wrong -- GitHub's fine-grained PAT model only exposes the `dispatches` endpoint under repository Contents: read & write, the same grant needed to push commits or write/delete files via the Contents API, so a leaked `ORACLE_STATUS_GH_TOKEN` is push-equivalent access to this repo, not a heartbeat-only capability; all three locations now state the true scope and the resulting handling expectation (rotate and guard it like any other write credential). Separately, `tests/unit/test_collect_oracle_status.py` never exercised `collect_oracle_status.py`'s own `main()` CLI (exit codes 0/1/2 for a fresh/replayed/unavailable result), unlike the matching convention already established for the sibling `collect_vps_status.py` -- added three `main()`-level tests covering all three outcomes. Full `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/*.sh` suite (14 files) and `tests/unit/test_collect_oracle_status.py`/`test_ops_status.py` (124 passed) reverified green; `ruff check`/`mypy` clean; `bash -n`/YAML parse clean on every touched script/workflow. Both required `/review-branch` passes are now clean on this re-synced, re-reviewed result. (PR #400)

- 2026-09-11: Item 120: added `scripts/collect_vps_status.py`, a read-only VPS-side collector for the `vps_loop` operations-status component (item 118's contract). Reuses `vps_loop_health.build_report` (item 117) for `current_item`/`blocker_class`/pause state/`repeated_without_progress`/`stale_pause` rather than re-deriving them, and adds facts only the VPS host itself can observe: `claude-loop.service`'s systemd `ActiveState`/`SubState` (only an explicit `failed` feeds `reported_failure`; any other query outcome degrades to `unknown`, never a fabricated failure), a tri-state `claude_process_state` (`running`/`absent`/`unknown` via `pgrep`, since `claude-loop.service` is a `Type=oneshot` unit whose process is normally absent between hourly ticks), branch/worktree/stash counts, and disk usage. `classify_loop_activity` distinguishes `active`/`restarting`/`paused`/`idle`/`unknown` by a fixed priority order (a running process wins outright; three same-tag blocked ticks in a row means `restarting` even with nothing running right now; then `paused`; then `unknown`; otherwise `idle`). `build_vps_loop_status` is a pure function over an already-gathered `VpsFacts` dataclass, kept separate from the actual systemd/process/git/disk I/O in `collect_vps_facts` so tests exercise every `healthy`/`degraded`/`stale`/`failed`/`unknown` state transition and every `loop_activity` bucket via fixtures, without mocking subprocess. New `tests/unit/test_collect_vps_status.py` (37 tests) also covers each gatherer's own degraded-input paths (systemctl/pgrep/git missing or failing, zero-size disk) and the CLI's exit codes. Review fix: `query_systemd_unit` checked only `ActiveState`/`SubState`/`Result`, but `systemctl show` exits 0 and reports `ActiveState=inactive`/`SubState=dead` even for a unit that was never installed -- indistinguishable from a legitimately idle, actually-installed unit without also checking `LoadState`, so a misconfigured `--service-unit` silently read as "idle" instead of degrading to `unknown` as the module's own docstring already claimed. Now also requests `LoadState` and treats anything other than `loaded` the same as a nonzero exit code; added a regression test. Also made `LOOP_ACTIVITY_STATES` load-bearing (asserted against `classify_loop_activity`'s own return value rather than left as a docstring-only reference) and corrected a comment overstating that the `stale` multiplier threshold is "the same point" `vps_loop_health`'s own `stale_pause` alert fires at, when that alert's `stale_pause_buffer` makes its threshold 1.5x later. Verified: `ruff check .`, canonical `poetry run mypy` (108 source files, scripts/tests aren't in its configured scope), and the full `tests/unit/` suite (949 passed) all clean; no DB touched. (PR #401)

- 2026-09-12: Item 134: `OnboardingGate.tsx` now redirects a genuinely first-time anonymous visitor from `/` to `/welcome` instead of rendering the dashboard or agency picker, giving the existing but previously unlinked `/welcome` page (`LandingPage.tsx`) a real audience. Added `frontend/src/api/welcomeSeen.ts` (`readWelcomeSeen`/`writeWelcomeSeen`, key `transit.welcomeSeen`), a dedicated localStorage flag deliberately separate from `lastAgency.ts` since "Continue as a guest" reaches `/` without ever picking an agency, so gating on `lastAgency` alone would loop straight back to `/welcome`. `OnboardingGate` checks `useSession()` (never redirects a signed-in visitor, regardless of the flag) and sets the flag unconditionally in a mount effect the moment it renders at all, so a browser is only ever bounced to `/welcome` once, however `/` is first reached (the welcome page's own guest link, a `/login` success, or a direct deep link). No new visible strings. Extended `OnboardingGate.test.tsx` with a `useSession` mock (defaulted to an already-past-the-gate anonymous visitor so every pre-existing picker test is unaffected) and a new "welcome redirect" suite covering the fresh-browser redirect, the flag surviving to a second visit, the signed-in bypass, and the already-seen bypass. Updated `docs/features/welcome-page.md` to describe the new gate instead of stating `/welcome` goes unseen by real users. Review fix: `readWelcomeSeen()` originally returned a plain `boolean`, collapsing "confirmed unseen" and "localStorage threw" into the same `false` -- since `OnboardingGate` redirects whenever `!session && !welcomeSeen`, a visitor whose browser can't read/write `localStorage` (storage blocked by privacy settings, an extension, or a locked-down environment) got `false` on every single mount forever, so `/` redirected to `/welcome` on every visit including the one right after clicking "Continue as a guest" -- an unbreakable loop that fully blocked the anonymous path, unlike the analogous `lastAgency` read failure which only degrades to "always show the agency picker." `readWelcomeSeen()` now returns a tri-state `WelcomeSeenState` (`"seen" | "unseen" | "unavailable"`), and `OnboardingGate` only redirects on a confirmed `"unseen"`; a `localStorage`-broken browser now falls through to the dashboard/picker like a `lastAgency` failure would, instead of looping. Added `frontend/src/api/welcomeSeen.test.ts` (mirroring `lastAgency.test.ts`, including a throwing-`getItem` case asserting `"unavailable"` rather than `"unseen"`) and an `OnboardingGate.test.tsx` regression case simulating a throwing `localStorage.getItem` for an anonymous visitor and asserting it lands on the agency picker rather than `/welcome`. Second review fix (same regression class, different trigger): a browser whose `setItem` always throws (or silently no-ops, e.g. quota already exhausted by other keys) while `getItem` keeps working reproduced the identical infinite loop, because `readWelcomeSeen()` only mapped a *read* failure to `"unavailable"` -- a write failure left the key permanently unwritten, so every later mount still read a genuine `"unseen"` and redirected again, forever, including right after "Continue as a guest". `writeWelcomeSeen()` now reads the key back immediately after writing it and, on a thrown or mismatched round-trip, sets a module-scoped in-memory flag that persists for the rest of this JS execution context (a full reload starts fresh and re-attempts real storage, which is all it needs to survive -- just the mounts within one tab session); `readWelcomeSeen()` reports `"unavailable"` instead of `"unseen"` once that flag is set, even though the underlying key is still absent. Added a test-only `resetWelcomeSeenMemoryForTests()` export so cases in the same test file don't leak that in-memory flag into each other, a `welcomeSeen.test.ts` case asserting the next read reports `"unavailable"` after a setItem-only failure, and an `OnboardingGate.test.tsx` case mirroring the existing getItem-throws regression but with only `setItem` throwing, rendering twice to confirm the second mount does not redirect. Updated `docs/features/welcome-page.md`'s "First-time redirect from `/`" section with a bullet documenting the storage-failure fallthrough and the write round-trip check. Third review fix: `useSession()` was destructured for `data`/`isLoading` only, so the redirect condition (`!session && welcomeSeenState === "unseen"`) treated any non-401 `/api/me` failure (5xx, aborted request, network error) the same as a confirmed-anonymous visitor -- react-query's `retry: 1` (`main.tsx`) settles such a query with `isLoading: false` and `data: undefined`, indistinguishable from real anonymity, so a signed-in visitor whose session probe merely hiccupped on a browser where the welcome flag was still unset (e.g. right after their first login on that device) could be misrouted to `/welcome` instead of the dashboard -- contradicting this item's own "never redirect a signed-in visitor... regardless of the flag's state" objective. `OnboardingGate` now also destructures `isError` from `useSession()` and requires `!isSessionError` before redirecting, falling through to the dashboard/picker on a session-check error the same way a `localStorage` read/write failure already falls through. Added an `OnboardingGate.test.tsx` case mocking `useSession()` to `{ data: undefined, isLoading: false, isError: true }` and asserting no redirect to `/welcome`. Verified: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test` (607 passed), `npm run lint` (0 errors, 4 pre-existing unrelated warnings), `npm run lint:i18n`, `npm run lint:i18n-strings`, `npm run test:check-entry-chunk`, and `npm run build:bundle && npm run check:entry-chunk` (MapLibre stays out of the entry chunk) all clean. No backend touched. (PR #418)
- 2026-09-12: Item 122: added `oracle_cloud/v3/bin/storage-metrics.sh`, a storage metrics collector reporting this VM's filesystem usage and Cloudflare R2 object count/bytes split by `rt`/`static` prefixes as an operations-status document (component `r2`), classified against configurable warning/critical thresholds (`DISK_WARN_PCT`/`DISK_CRIT_PCT`, `R2_BYTES_WARN_THRESHOLD`/`R2_BYTES_CRIT_THRESHOLD`). Unlike `status-snapshot.sh`, it needs `OBJECT_STORE_*` credentials of its own to list R2 directly (nothing else already computes a whole-bucket total); missing credentials report `r2_state=not_applicable` rather than failing, and each prefix is listed independently via `aws s3api list-objects-v2 --no-paginate` with the script following `NextToken` itself so a partial listing failure reports the failed prefix as null without hiding behind the other's success. `.storage-metrics.last-success` preserves the last genuine success's timestamp across a failed run, mirroring `verify-r2.sh`'s marker split. The existing generic `bin/publish-status.sh` sends this document to GitHub too (pointed at its own output file/event type via env, not a new publisher script); added `.github/workflows/r2-storage-heartbeat-listener.yml` and `scripts/collect_r2_status.py` as the GitHub-side/VPS-side legs, structurally mirroring the oracle_crawler heartbeat channel (items 118/119) but keyed on component `"r2"` with an independent replay watermark. New `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/test_storage_metrics.sh` (24 cases: threshold boundaries inclusive at warn/crit, multi-page pagination via NextToken, a page-count safety cap, missing credentials, and partial rt/static listing failures) and `tests/unit/test_collect_r2_status.py` (mirroring `test_collect_oracle_status.py`'s replay/contract-validation coverage). Verified: all 15 `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/test_*.sh` files pass (176 cases total, including the new suite); `python3 scripts/ops_status.py --validate` accepts every document shape this collector can produce; manual smoke-tests of `collect_r2_status.py`'s parse/replay/watermark logic against `scripts/ops_status.py` (no poetry virtualenv provisioned in this sandbox, so `poetry run pytest`/`ruff`/`mypy` could not be run here -- flagging for the follow-up verification pass). Review fix: the described `aws s3api list-objects-v2 --no-paginate` + manual `--starting-token` pagination is rejected outright by real AWS CLI v2 (`--no-paginate` and `--starting-token` cannot be combined), so any prefix over one page would have permanently reported `r2_state=failed` in production -- a failure mode the bundled test's fake `aws` shim wrongly accepted, hiding it. `r2_list_prefix()` now mirrors `spool-cleanup.sh`'s own `r2_list()`: one `aws s3 ls --recursive --endpoint-url ...` call per prefix (which paginates internally), with object count/bytes summed from its `<date> <time> <size> <key>` output lines; the `STORAGE_METRICS_MAX_PAGES` variable, its validation, and the manual `NextToken` loop are gone entirely, and a listing failure now logs the actual captured `aws` stderr instead of a generic message. `test_storage_metrics.sh`'s fake `aws` shim now responds to `aws s3 ls --recursive` with fixture listing lines; the NextToken/three-page/page-cap tests were removed and replaced with a multi-object-summation case and a listing-failure case asserting the captured stderr reaches the script's own logged output. Also deleted a dead assertion (`grep -c ... "$TEST_BASE/../aws.log" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true`) that read the wrong path and discarded its own exit status. Reverified: `bash -n` clean on both changed `.sh` files; the full `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/test_storage_metrics.sh` suite (24 cases) and all 15 `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/*.sh` files pass; `scripts/collect_r2_status.py` was untouched by this fix (no Python surface). Pass 2 fix: publish-status: ORACLE_STATUS_GH_TOKEN is not set — status publishing is disabled, skipping (a generic script this diff makes serve a second component, r2 alongside oracle_crawler) still hardcoded `oracle_crawler` in its own success log line, so every r2 heartbeat publish logged a self-contradictory "published oracle_crawler status ... (event r2-storage-heartbeat)"; now derives the component name from the published document itself. Reverified: all 15 `oracle_cloud/v3/tests/*.sh` files (including `test_publish_status.sh`) pass. (PR #417)
- 2026-09-12: Item 126: made the gitleaks pre-commit hook the mandatory local first line of defense against committed secrets. Switched `.pre-commit-config.yaml` from the `gitleaks` hook id (`language: golang`, which builds gitleaks from source via a local Go toolchain on first use) to `gitleaks-system`, which just runs the pinned binary on PATH. Added `scripts/setup_git_hooks.sh`, which installs the pinned gitleaks release and pre-commit itself if missing, then verifies the installed `.git/hooks/pre-commit` is executable, carries pre-commit's own generated marker, and isn't shadowed by a `core.hooksPath` override -- it fails loudly on any of these instead of silently skipping. Wired it into `make bootstrap` (now fails the whole target if the hook can't be installed, replacing a soft warn-and-continue) and a new standalone `make hooks`/`make doctor` status line; `git worktree`s share one `.git/hooks` directory, so one run against a persistent clone covers every `/vps-loop-run` worker worktree cut from it. `deploy/vps/claude-loop.sh` now checks and lazily (re)installs the hook every tick, failing closed like its other provisioning checks. Documented the mandatory hook and `make hooks` in README.md (workstation) and `.claude/README.md` (VPS). Added a repository-safe positive-control fixture (`tests/fixtures/secrets/positive_control_credential.txt`, AWS's own published non-functional example access key) and a negative-control fixture for an approved, allowlisted placeholder (`negative_control_allowed_placeholder.txt`); both are also listed in `.gitleaks.toml`'s own allowlist so they don't perpetually fail the routine full-repo scan (`make verify-secrets`, CI's `secrets-scan.yml`), while `tests/unit/test_gitleaks_fixtures.py` re-scans each with a bare, allowlist-free config to prove gitleaks' ruleset genuinely detects-and-redacts the positive control and would flag the negative control too absent the allowlist. `tests/unit/test_gitleaks_version_pin.py` asserts the gitleaks version stays identical across `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, `secrets-scan.yml`, and the setup script (a `pre-commit` `rev:` can't reference a shared file). `tests/unit/test_setup_git_hooks.py` runs the real setup script against a throwaway git repo with stub `gitleaks`/`pre-commit` binaries on a curated, installer-free PATH, proving it fails when no installer is reachable, when an installed hook lacks pre-commit's marker, and when `core.hooksPath` would bypass it -- and succeeds and installs a real, verifiable hook otherwise. Also added `scripts/setup_git_hooks.sh` to `prepare_review.py`'s `ENFORCEMENT_PATTERNS` (and its test), matching the existing `.pre-commit-config.yaml` entry. Verified: manually ran all new/changed tests (69 assertions across four new test modules) against a locally downloaded pinned gitleaks v8.18.4 binary with a minimal pytest-marker stub, all passing; `python3 -m py_compile` clean on every touched Python file; `bash -n` clean on both changed shell scripts; `make -n`/`make doctor` dry-run confirmed the Makefile wiring; a full working-tree gitleaks scan of the final diff is clean. This sandbox has no provisioned poetry virtualenv, `pip`, or network-reachable installer (`ModuleNotFoundError: No module named pip`), so `poetry run pytest`/`ruff`/`mypy` could not be run directly here -- flagging for the follow-up verification pass. (PR #419)

- 2026-09-12: Item 128: `deploy/vps/claude-loop.sh` now chains `/vps-loop-run` ticks back to back within one invocation instead of relying on the next hourly timer firing for each item. New `scripts/vps_loop_chain_state.py` persists small wrapper-only bookkeeping (distinct from `NEXT_TASK.md`'s own Status log) at `/root/vps-loop-chain-state.json` and exposes `gate` (may a new tick start now -- also recovers a chain-state file left stuck `in_progress` by a wrapper killed mid-tick, e.g. by systemd's own `TimeoutStartSec`, folding that recovery in as one more non-progress occurrence), `begin`, and `record-outcome` (decide `continue` vs. `stop`, escalating a capped exponential backoff -- `CLAUDE_LOOP_BACKOFF_BASE_SEC`/`CLAUDE_LOOP_BACKOFF_CAP_SEC`, default 300s/3600s -- on every non-progress outcome). `scripts/vps_loop_health.py` gained `compute_last_tick_outcome`/`last_tick_outcome` (`"progress"`/`"idle"`/`"blocked"`/`"paused"`/`"unknown"`, read from the single most recent Status log entry) as the signal the wrapper chains on. The chain is bounded on every axis: `CLAUDE_LOOP_MAX_CHAIN_TICKS` (default 5), `CLAUDE_LOOP_MAX_CHAIN_WALLCLOCK_SEC` (default 14400s, wrapper-enforced independent of systemd), `CLAUDE_TICK_TIMEOUT_SEC` (default 3300s, unchanged -- still the cap on any individual tick), and `CLAUDE_LOOP_CHAIN_DELAY_SEC` (default 5s) between chained ticks as an API-rate-limit courtesy delay. The outer single-flight `flock` is unchanged and still the sole guarantee against two overlapping wrapper processes; `deploy/systemd/claude-loop.service`'s `TimeoutStartSec` was raised from 3300s to 18600s to fit a full chain, which deliberately retires the old "timer cadence exceeds the wrapper's hard timeout" invariant (documented in `.claude/README.md`). `scripts/collect_vps_status.py` and `vps-heartbeat-listener.yml` now also surface `last_tick_outcome` and the chain-state fields (`chain_in_progress`/`chain_consecutive_non_progress`/`chain_next_earliest_attempt`/`chain_last_outcome` and per-heartbeat `chain_tick_outcome`/`chain_ticks_run`). Added `deploy/vps/claude-loop.sh`, `deploy/systemd/claude-loop.service`, and `scripts/vps_loop_chain_state.py` (plus its test) to `scripts/prepare_review.py`'s `ENFORCEMENT_PATTERNS`, since this machinery now gates how aggressively the loop can merge PRs unattended. Verified: `poetry run pytest tests/unit/test_vps_loop_chain_state.py tests/unit/test_vps_loop_health.py tests/unit/test_collect_vps_status.py tests/unit/test_prepare_review.py` (all passing, including new success-chaining/failure-stop/stale-lock-recovery/no-actionable-work-backoff cases), full `poetry run pytest tests/unit/` (1032 passed, 26 skipped, pre-existing/unrelated), `poetry run ruff check` and `poetry run mypy` clean on every touched Python file, `bash -n` clean on `deploy/vps/claude-loop.sh`. (PR #pending)
- 2026-09-12: Item 128, review-fix (pass 1): fixed a Major and four Minors from `/review-branch` Pass 1. Major: `deploy/vps/claude-loop.sh` pre-declared `CHAIN_`-prefixed local placeholders (`CHAIN_NEXT_EARLIEST_ATTEMPT`/`CHAIN_CONSECUTIVE_NON_PROGRESS`) before `eval`-ing `record-outcome`'s shell output, but that command emits the unprefixed `NEXT_EARLIEST_ATTEMPT`/`CONSECUTIVE_NON_PROGRESS` -- so the wrapper's two heartbeat fields (`chain_consecutive_non_progress`/`chain_next_earliest_attempt`) were always empty; renamed the wrapper's locals to match (Python side, and `cmd_gate`/`cmd_show`'s own use of the same names, left untouched). Minors: threaded `--backoff-base-seconds`/`--backoff-cap-seconds` through `gate_parser`/`cmd_gate`/`recover_if_stale` so a recovered stale lock honors the operator's configured backoff instead of `record_outcome`'s hardcoded defaults, and updated the wrapper's `gate` invocation to pass them; removed `default_pid_alive`'s dead `except OSError: errno.ESRCH` branch (unreachable behind the preceding `ProcessLookupError` clause) and the now-unused `errno` import; dropped the `(item 128)` parenthetical from the wrapper's banner comment per this repo's no-issue-numbers-in-source rule; removed the unreferenced `SHIPPED_MARKERS` constant from `scripts/vps_loop_health.py`. Also applied the low-confidence optional fix: clamped `compute_backoff_seconds`'s exponent before raising `2 **` it, so an extreme `consecutive_non_progress` streak can no longer risk `OverflowError` before the existing cap applies. Added regression tests: a wrapper/Python variable-name cross-check that would have caught the Major, custom-backoff-params coverage for `recover_if_stale`/the `gate` CLI, and an extreme-streak case for `compute_backoff_seconds`. Verified: `poetry run pytest tests/unit/test_vps_loop_chain_state.py tests/unit/test_vps_loop_health.py` (73 passed), `poetry run ruff check` and `poetry run mypy` clean on every touched Python file, `bash -n` clean on `deploy/vps/claude-loop.sh`. (PR #pending)
- 2026-09-12: Item 128, review-fix (pass 2): fixed one Major and two Minors from a second `/review-branch`-style pass. Major: a gated/denied invocation (`ALLOWED != "true"`) previously only logged and `exit 0`'d without ever sending a `vps-heartbeat` repository-dispatch event -- the only place a heartbeat goes out -- so once `CLAUDE_LOOP_BACKOFF_CAP_SEC` (default 3600s, exactly the hourly timer cadence) saturated during an ordinary idle/blocked backlog stretch, the loop settled into a roughly 120-minute heartbeat cadence that exceeds `vps-heartbeat-watchdog.yml`'s 100-minute freshness threshold, producing a recurring false "loop may have stopped" alarm. `deploy/vps/claude-loop.sh` now factors the health-gathering and dispatch call into two small shared functions (`collect_vps_loop_health`/`dispatch_heartbeat`), called from both the per-tick loop body and the gated-exit path (which reports a `chain_tick_outcome` of `gated`, `chain_ticks_run` of 0, and the gate's own consecutive-non-progress/next-earliest-attempt fields since no tick ran). Added `test_wrapper_gated_exit_still_dispatches_a_heartbeat` (`tests/unit/test_vps_loop_chain_state.py`), a source-text regression guard in the same style as the file's existing wrapper/Python variable-name cross-check, asserting the single dispatch call site lives inside `dispatch_heartbeat` and that the gated-exit branch calls it before its own `exit 0`. Minor: added `scripts/vps_loop_health.py` and `tests/unit/test_vps_loop_health.py` to `scripts/prepare_review.py`'s `ENFORCEMENT_PATTERNS` (with matching `tests/unit/test_prepare_review.py` parametrize cases) -- `compute_last_tick_outcome` is the sole classifier feeding the wrapper's continue/stop decision and had been left out of the enforcement tier despite `scripts/vps_loop_chain_state.py` already being covered for the same underlying reason. Minor: corrected `scripts/vps_loop_chain_state.py`'s module docstring, which undercounted its CLI subcommands as three (`gate`/`begin`/`record-outcome`) and omitted the fourth, `show` (a read-only manual-inspection command the wrapper itself never calls). Verified: `poetry run pytest tests/unit/test_vps_loop_chain_state.py tests/unit/test_vps_loop_health.py tests/unit/test_prepare_review.py tests/unit/test_collect_vps_status.py` (143 passed) against throwaway `:5544`/`:8124`; `poetry run ruff check` and `poetry run mypy` clean on every touched Python file (`scripts/vps_loop_chain_state.py`, `scripts/prepare_review.py`, `tests/unit/test_prepare_review.py`, `tests/unit/test_vps_loop_chain_state.py`); `bash -n deploy/vps/claude-loop.sh` clean. Not built: an automated test that actually invokes the wrapper shell script end-to-end with a mocked `gh`/`claude` (no such harness exists in this repo yet, and building one from scratch was judged disproportionate to this fix -- the added test instead statically verifies the wrapper's source structure, matching the existing precedent already in this same file).
